## One-step install: npm launcher, server.json, launch commands (2026-11-07)

- Changed: `packaging/npm/` (package `phonedriver`, `bin/phonedriver.js`, `lib/launcher.js`, README, 5 offline tests), `server.json`, `docs/release/registry.md` (research with sources), `docs/release/launch-commands.md`, D29 in `docs/decisions.md`, README and site install pages (npx first, installers as permanent alternatives), `docs/launch/directories.md` section 1, launch drafts' install line. No MCPB bundle: the manifest has no architecture field (D29). Nothing published, pushed or created remotely.
- Commands and output: `node --test packaging/npm/test/*.test.js` 5 pass. `npm pack` then `npm i` of the tarball in a temp dir, with a local `.tar.xz` of `target/release/phonedriver` and its `.sha256` served as a `file://` release: `npx -y phonedriver --version` printed the first-run line on stderr and `phonedriver 0.1.0`; a second run printed no download line; `npx phonedriver mcp` with an `initialize` request on stdin answered `serverInfo` name `phonedriver`, version `0.1.0`. `server.json` validates against the 2025-12-11 schema (ajv, draft-07) and `mcp-publisher validate` (v1.8.8) says valid. `python3 site/build.py` and `site/check.py`: problems 0.
- Found: dist archives are `.tar.xz` on Unix and Node cannot read xz, so the launcher shells out to `tar`. `mcp-publisher validate` checks the schema and contacts the registry but cannot check that the npm package exists. The cached binary has an ad hoc arm64 signature and no quarantine attribute.
- Not tested: Linux, Windows and macOS x64 archives (never built), a clean Mac with Gatekeeper, the real GitHub download path (only `file://`), `mcp-publisher publish`. The binary used was the existing `target/release/phonedriver`, not rebuilt.

## Cable-free Android: `pair` (QR) and `untether` (2026-11-08)

- Changed: `crates/android/src/wireless.rs` (QR payload, `host:mdns:services` parser, `ip -o -4 addr show` parser, interface labels), `Adb::{mdns_check, mdns_services, pair, connect_device, tcpip}` in `crates/android/src/adb.rs`, `crates/cli/src/wireless.rs` (the two flows, QR rendering), the `pair` and `untether` verbs, doctor hints (`phonedriver pair` in the offline-Wi-Fi and no-device fixes), `qrcode` 0.14.1 (no default features), `docs/reference.md` (QR first, code pairing as fallback, "Away from your Wi-Fi"), D30. Not added to README, the site or SKILL.md (the parent does that after a physical phone test). Both commands are marked "not yet tested on a physical phone" in docs and `--help`.
- Formats come from adb's source, not from guesses: `client/transport_mdns.cpp` `mdns_list_discovered_services` prints `instance\tservice\tip:port\n` (the Bonjour resolver adds `._tcp.`, so both are accepted); `services.cpp` `pair_service` sends the success line as a protocol string and the failure as `FAIL` + hex length + text after `OKAY`; `commandline.cpp` builds `host:pair:<code>:<addr>`; `adb mdns services` prints the `List of discovered mdns services` header in the client, not the server.
- Tests (written before the parsers in the android crate: 6 red on `todo!()`, then green): `blaze test -p phonedriver-android wireless` 7 passed (QR payload, OpenScreen and Bonjour listings, the real listing below, empty and header-only text, a real API 37 `ip -o -4 addr show`, labels); `--test adb_fake_server` 13 passed (4 new: pair success, pair failure framing, mdns check/services/connect, tcpip); `blaze test -p phonedriver --lib` 47 passed (7 new in `wireless`: pairing service chosen by our name, connect service must come from the same IP, already-connected phone found by address or mDNS name, device pick, `connected to`/`already connected to`, QR light quiet zone and dark finder corner, black-on-white on a terminal; the doctor test now also asserts the `phonedriver pair` hint). Not test-first, said honestly: the CLI `wireless` tests and the doctor assertion were written together with the code because the first run failed on a full disk before it could show red.
- Real runs, adb 37.0.1 on the Mac:
  - `phonedriver pair` prints a QR; piped through `swift` (CIDetector QR on the half-block text, 8x scale) it decodes to `WIFI:T:ADB;S:phonedriver-GCFC3W;P:42M65FMMAA42;;`. With nobody scanning, exit 3 after 2 minutes with the same-Wi-Fi / mDNS / pairing-code message.
  - `dns-sd -R phonedriver-TEST _adb-tls-pairing._tcp . 37099` then `adb mdns services`: `phonedriver-TEST	_adb-tls-pairing._tcp	10.31.234.154:37099` (now a parser test).
  - `host:mdns:check` answers `mdns daemon version [adb discovery 0.0.0]`; `host:pair:123456:127.0.0.1:9` answers `OKAY` then `FAIL0027Failed: Unable to start pairing client.`; `host:connect:127.0.0.1:9` answers the text `failed to connect to '127.0.0.1:9': Connection refused`.
  - `untether` on `emulator-5558` (win-ts adb server through `ssh -L 5041:127.0.0.1:5037`): after `adb usb`, `service.adb.tcp.port` was `0`; `phonedriver untether --device android:emulator-5558` printed `10.0.2.15 eth0`, `10.0.2.18 wlan0 (Wi-Fi)`, tried both, and failed in 18.5 s with exit 1 and the "put this computer on the same network" message, because the emulator's 10.0.2.x network is not reachable from the host; afterwards the property was `5555`, and a `adb emu redir` forward to guest port 5555 (set on win-ts) accepted `adb connect`. `adb usb` restored `0`. The first run, before the per-address timeout, took 43 s (21 s per unreachable address on Windows); the 8 s limit per address is that observation.
- Found: an emulator's adbd answers on 5555 even in USB mode, so the redir connect does not prove `tcpip` by itself; the property change `0` to `5555` and the device's `restarting in TCP mode port: 5555` answer do. `phonedriver doctor` prints the adb server as `0029 at 127.0.0.1:5037` (the hex length is shown, not the version; not fixed here). `target/rust-analyzer` (2 GB, blaze's own cache) refilled the disk repeatedly and deleting it crashed a blaze worker; the disk was 99% full during this task. `blaze test` on a worktree can answer `crate .../hidden_display.rs not found in workspace` when another worktree's worker is busy; retrying works.
- Not proven, needs a physical Android 11+ phone: the QR scan itself and the phone advertising `_adb-tls-pairing` with the QR's name as instance name; the success reply of `host:pair`; whether adb auto-connects before or after `pair` returns and what serial it uses (`adb-<id>._adb-tls-connect._tcp` or `ip:port`; the code accepts both and otherwise runs `connect`); `host:mdns:check` failing text when mDNS is off; `untether` reaching a real address (Wi-Fi and Tailscale) and the labels of a real `tun0`.

## Guard: no plain `type` into password and one-time code fields (2026-11-08)

- Changed: `core::error::Error::SecretField` (`secret_field`, -32020) and `SecretKind`; `daemon/secret_field.rs` (`kind(&Element)`: `SecureField` or the password flag is a password; a text field whose label, hint or resource id names a one-time code is a code, in eleven languages); `actor.rs` refuses a non-empty, non-`secret` `type` into either before any tap or key; `mcp.rs` rewrites the hint's `--secret` to `secret: true`. SKILL.md, protocol, architecture, the site's approval-guard, Claude Code and install pages, README, D31.
- What the element model exposes (checked in `core/src/element.rs`, `raw.rs`): role, label (content description on Android), hint (placeholder), id (resource id), the password flag. No input type, no autofill hint. So one-time codes are matched on words only, and a bare `code` label or hint counts only when it is the whole text (`Promo code`, `Zip code`, `Barcode` and `优惠码` do not).
- Tests first (red: did not compile without `SecretField`): in `tests/daemon.rs` `plain_text_is_refused_in_a_password_field_and_nothing_is_typed` (no `Text` or `Gesture` call, the text in neither the error nor the trace), `plain_text_is_refused_in_the_focused_password_field_and_clearing_it_is_not`, `plain_text_is_refused_in_a_one_time_code_field_but_a_secret_goes_in` (17 label, hint and id variants in 10 languages, each then typed with `secret` and `PasteSecret` sent), `plain_text_still_goes_into_fields_that_only_mention_a_code` (13 promo, zip, postal, barcode variants), `a_batch_refuses_plain_text_for_a_password_field_and_says_what_to_do`; `typing_a_secret_into_a_password_field_is_verified_by_length` replaces the old test that expected plain text to be masked; core `every_variant_serializes_with_code_name_message_and_hint` covers both kinds; the MCP hint rewrite is in `hints_name_tools_and_arguments`. The two device tests that typed plain text into the testbed's password field (`actions.rs`, `e2e.rs`) now expect the refusal.
- Command and output: `cargo test -p phonedriver-core -p phonedriver` all `test result: ok` (daemon 109 passed, guard 30, core 149); `cargo clippy ... --tests` and `cargo fmt --check` clean; `site/build.py` and `site/check.py` `problems 0`.
- Found: the jobs benchmark's `qa-signup` job fills a password field, so a rerun needs the agent to pass `secret`; `qa-shop` types the promo code `SAVE10`, which is not refused. I did not rerun the jobs benchmark.
- Not done: card number, CVV and PIN fields; an input-type or autofill-hint read from the Android agent (needs a new field in the wire).

## 2026-11-08: `phonedriver --device X mcp` pins the MCP server to X

- Found while recording the Domino's demo: with three emulators on one adb server, Claude was asked which device to use although the server was started with `--device emulator-5554`. `Verb::Mcp => crate::mcp::run()` dropped the global flag (`mcp` listed `device` among its ignored globals); only `PHONEDRIVER_DEVICE` worked.
- Changed: `mcp` keeps `--device`; `Mcp::pinned(device)`; `globals(&self, ..)` takes a call's own `device`, then the pinned one, then `PHONEDRIVER_DEVICE`. Tests `a_pinned_server_sends_calls_that_name_no_device_to_its_device` (did not compile before: no `pinned`) and `the_mcp_server_takes_the_device_flag` (flag before and after the verb).
- Also merged from agent worktrees: `pair`/`untether` (D30) and the guard's languages, opt-in categories and secret fields, renumbered D31 because both agents took D30.
- Evidence: `blaze test -p phonedriver`: every suite `ok`, 0 failed (lib 55 passed, daemon 109, guard 28). Demo recording driver `design/build/demo_record.py` gained scene 3 (one continuous Domino's run) and passes `PHONEDRIVER_DEVICE`.

## 2026-11-08: a held step inside `batch` asks the human, like a tap

- Found recording the Domino's demo: Claude put the tap on the real "Buy with GPay" in a `batch`. The guard held it, but `batch` in the MCP server ran outside `guarded`, so no prompt reached Claude Code; the model got "Ask the user in chat" twice and the demo never showed the approval prompt. Only `tap` and `type` were guarded in MCP.
- Changed: `Mcp::guarded_batch` (both protocols; the 2026-07-28 path keeps `held_batch` keyed by the batch's device, steps and timeout) asks with the same prompt, and after Allow runs the batch again from the held step, so the steps before it run once. The daemon's `batch` gives a once/session approval to its first step only, so an Allow covers the one step it was asked for; `run_batch` passes once/session and adds the 1-based `step` to the refusal's data.
- Tests (crates/cli/tests/guard.rs, legacy and mrtr each): `a_held_step_in_a_batch_is_asked_and_the_batch_resumes_there` (gestures 2, one prompt), `declining_a_held_batch_step_stops_the_batch_there` (gestures 1), `an_approval_in_a_batch_covers_only_the_step_it_was_asked_for` (two prompts, gestures 3). Red before the change: `test result: FAILED. 30 passed; 6 failed`. After: `cargo test -p phonedriver`: every suite ok, 0 failed (guard 36 passed, lib 55, daemon 109).

## 2026-11-08: typing into web fields: a refused paste falls back to keys, and late text is waited for

- Found in the Domino's demo transcript: (1) `type` into dominos.co.uk's postcode field reported `NOT verified: @e15 shows no text` three times (the field was focused, keyboard up; the page takes keystrokes but not the paste; by hand `--mode keys` worked). (2) In a batch, `type "Sam"` reported `NOT verified` while the batch's own diff showed `@e789 → "Sam"`: Chrome exposed the value after the verify read.
- Changed (`daemon/actor.rs`): when no `mode` was given, the field is in a web view, the text is not secret, nothing is submitted and the field is still empty after the paste, the same text is typed key by key and verified again (`via: keys`). `report` re-reads a web field for up to 1.5 s (`WEB_TEXT_WAIT`) before calling typing unverified. An explicit `mode: paste` is never retried another way. Test hook `FakeDevice::on_text`.
- Tests (`crates/cli/tests/daemon.rs`): `a_web_field_that_ignores_a_paste_gets_the_text_key_by_key`, `a_paste_asked_for_by_name_is_not_retried_another_way`, `text_that_shows_in_a_web_field_a_moment_late_is_verified`. With the actor change reverted: `FAILED. 1 passed; 2 failed`; restored: `ok. 3 passed`. Full: `cargo test -p phonedriver -p phonedriver-core`: 26 suites ok, 0 failed; clippy `-D warnings` clean.

## Hidden second screen on Android 13+ (2026-11-08)

- Changed: `--display hidden` and `PHONEDRIVER_DISPLAY=hidden` (D32, plan `docs/plans/2026-11-08-10-hidden-display.md`). The agent gets a third argument `hidden`, creates the encoder and a virtual display with scrcpy 3.x's flags (`Hidden.java`) and reports `hidden_display`; windows, the focused input, injected events, `launch`/`open_url`, foreground, power and video are scoped to that id. The request carries `display`; the actor reconnects the device in the other mode. `devices` shows a DISPLAY column, `doctor` a `display` line, `health` the id. `press lock` and `press notifications` are `Unsupported` in hidden mode. Below API 33 the agent exits 5 and the call fails `Unsupported` for `display`. Also `bench/speed/run_display.py`, `docs/bench/hidden-display.md`, a live test in `crates/android/tests/device_live.rs`.
- Host tests (first): `cargo test -p phonedriver --test hidden_display` 5 pass (hidden connect and reconnect back, a source without support answers `Unsupported`, `devices` and `health` carry the id). `blaze test` skips this new test file (`crate crates/cli/tests/hidden_display.rs not found in workspace`), so cargo ran it.
- Proof, `emulator-5558` (API 37 tablet, win-ts), release build, display 80, 1280x800 pt: `launch dev.phonedriver.testbed`, `tap @e3` (Text tab), `type "typed on the hidden screen" --into @e42` (verified), `press hide-keyboard`, `tap @e45` (Send). Testbed event log (`logcat -s PUTB:I`, saved as `docs/bench/data/hidden-display-2026-11-08/testbed-events.log`): `{"ev":"tab","name":"text"}`, `{"ev":"text","field":"plain","value":"typed on the hidden screen"}`, `{"ev":"click","id":"send"}`. Earlier in the session `tap`, `swipe`, `scroll`, `press back`, `press home`, `clipboard set` and `clipboard` all worked on it (`{"ev":"tap","id":"b1",...}`), and the on-screen keyboard opened on the hidden display (the `Send` button sat under it; a tap there hit the keyboard's `b`, as it would on the main screen).
- Screenshots, looked at: `docs/bench/data/hidden-display-2026-11-08/hidden-screen.png` (the testbed's Text tab with the typed text, taken with `phonedriver screenshot`) and `main-during.png` against `main-before.png` (`screencap -d 4619827259835644672`): the launcher, widgets and dock are the same; the only difference above the status bar is the analog clock widget's hands (one minute apart).
- API 26 (`emulator-5556`): `phonedriver --device emulator-5556 --display hidden snapshot` printed `error: display is not supported on this device: a hidden display needs Android 13 (API 33) or newer, and the phone must allow it`, exit 1.
- Bench (`docs/bench/hidden-display.md`, 2 runs x 40 samples, same emulator, release build): p50 ms main against hidden, run 1 snapshot 129.4/116.7, tap 258.4/220.6, screenshot 15.9/12.1; run 2 67.0/70.4, 224.8/228.0, 9.6/9.8. Run 1 had two hidden snapshots near 1 s in the first hidden block; they did not return in run 2.
- Found, not fixed: (1) a screenshot taken right after a tap shows the frame before it for 0.4 to 1.6 s, in main mode too (6 trials each mode, `phonedriver screenshot` at 0.4 s steps); (2) `launch` in hidden mode says `not settled after 7.5 to 9 s` on the first call of a connection (agent start plus encoder), 1 to 3 s after; (3) the secondary-display launcher has no accessibility tree, so `snapshot` after `press home` is OCR text only; (4) `adb shell am start --display 80` is refused with `SecurityException`; (5) `doctor` probes every connected Android device in the requested mode.
- Mistake to note: I ran `PHONEDRIVER_DISPLAY=hidden phonedriver doctor` once to look at the display line. It probes every device on the shared adb server, so it started and released a hidden agent on `emulator-5554` (the demo emulator) and on `emulator-5556` for a few seconds; a demo agent already running there would have been replaced. Stopped my daemon straight after; no virtual display or agent was left on any of the three emulators (`dumpsys display`, `ps`).
- Not proven: physical phones, HONOR and Xiaomi builds, API 33 (no `OWN_FOCUS`), apps that force an orientation, a long session.

## 2026-11-08: web pages read in about 0.6 s, bound by Chrome, not by the node count (no change kept)

- Measured on dominos.co.uk's checkout (emulator-5554, API 37, Chrome): `snapshot` tree phase 544 to 620 ms; `adb shell uiautomator dump` of the same page 752 to 1060 ms. A `type` there took 7.6 to 8.8 s (paste 1.2 to 2.3 s including the clipboard restore wait, tree reads 1 to 2.5 s, stabilize 1.4 to 1.7 s).
- Tried: not walking below a node that is off the screen and invisible to the user. The raw tree then had 403 nodes (245 visible); tree phase 657 to 831 ms over 5 reads, no better. The cost is Chrome answering accessibility requests (about 1.6 ms a node over roughly 8 prefetch batches), so the change was reverted (no speculative code).
- Live check of the typing fix on the same build: `type --into "Enter your postcode" "WC2H 7LU"` on dominos.co.uk: `ok typed 8 chars into @e90 · verified · settled in 1901ms` (the field that took none of three pastes in the demo run).
- Open: tree reads on heavy web pages; the next idea is keeping the accessibility cache between reads instead of clearing it (it is cleared on every read today), which needs proof that events invalidate what changed.
## 2026-11-08: approval guard verified by property tests; `.claude/skills/verify-approval-guard`

- Changed (tests and a skill only, no product code): `crates/cli/tests/guard_property.rs` (new), `crates/cli/tests/support/guard_rig.rs` (the screens and daemon rig moved out of `tests/guard.rs`, which now imports it), `.claude/skills/verify-approval-guard/{SKILL.md,breaks.py}` (added with `git add -f`; `.claude/` is ignored). Tool: proptest (already a dev-dependency) on the real daemon, the real `Mcp` server and the fake device's input log; the oracle restates D27/D31 and shares no code with `mcp.rs`.
- What it checks: a generated session of 1 to 3 calls (`batch` of 1 to 5 steps with taps, waits and `type --submit`; single `tap`; single `type --submit`; some `autonomous`) against a scripted human (allow, session, decline, cancel, bare accept, junk, or a client that cannot ask), a generated ask set, on protocol 2025-11-25 and on 2026-07-28. The device's input log and the prompts shown must equal what the promise allows. A second property drives 2026-07-28 with raw `call_tool_once` and hand-built `input_responses`: questions answered twice, answered for the wrong batch, answered with none open. A third runs every answer kind against 5 held inputs on both protocols.
- Command and output: `cargo test -p phonedriver --test guard_property` -> `test result: ok. 3 passed; 0 failed; 4 ignored` in 29 s to 50 s. One run's counters: 115 sessions (70 of them the exhaustive table), 222 calls, 187 questions, 215 device inputs; retry property 46 sequences, 246 calls, 106 fresh questions, 41 exact resumes, 66 answers with no question open, 22 refused. `cargo test -p phonedriver --test guard` still `36 passed`; `cargo clippy -p phonedriver --tests` and `cargo fmt --all --check` clean.
- Fails on broken code (each applied to the product code, run, restored with `git checkout`; outputs from `.claude/skills/verify-approval-guard/breaks.py`): approval on every batch step (B1), resume from step 0 on the open connection (B2) and on 2026-07-28 (B2b), `held_batch` never cleared (B3, only the retry property sees it), any retry resumes the held batch (B4, retry property), session approval covers every category (B5), decline taps in a batch (B6, B6b), `type --submit` not guarded (B7), opt-in categories ignored (B8), a dismissed prompt taps a single tap (B9). Example, B3: `Call(0)` asks, `Retry(0, Allow)` runs `Place order, Send, Install`, a second `Retry(0, Allow)` runs all three again; the promise allows nothing. Example, B1: batch `[type --submit, Place order, Install, Place order]` with ask `install` and one Allow: the device pressed all four.
- The first scratch break (B9) was missed by the random property in the skill's own dry run (a rare answer on a rare path). Fixed in the verification, not in the break: more weight on cancel and decline, and the exhaustive table, which catches B9 in 3.5 s.
- Findings, product unchanged:
  - **F1.** On 2026-07-28 a `tap` or `type` retry is approved by whatever answer it carries; the server keeps no record of the question. `type {submit: true}` or `tap "Place order"` with an Allow answer and no question ever asked presses the button; an answer to a question about Place order approves a retry that names Delete photo. `batch` is bound to its question and is not affected. Needs a client that sends answers it was not given (the model cannot set `input_responses`). Reproduced by `f1_*` (ignored tests).
  - **F2.** An answer for batch B empties `held_batch` before the key is compared, so batch A's open question is lost and A's answer is dismissed; nothing is tapped, the human must be asked again (`f2_*`).
  - **F3.** The `mrtr::` half of `tests/guard.rs` ran the 2025-11-25 path: `.serve(..)` negotiates the old handshake whatever `get_info` names. With `ClientLifecycleMode::Discover`, 34 of 36 pass; 2 fail on text only (a decline or dismissal on a retry omits the button label and category). `guard_property.rs` uses Discover.
- Not covered: label classification in other languages and screens, concurrent calls, `PHONEDRIVER_GUARD=off`, the CLI path, taps by other routes (`shell input`, deep links), a real Claude Code client. Details in the skill.
- Deeper run: `PROPTEST_CASES=60 cargo test -p phonedriver --test guard_property` -> 3 passed in 201 s; retry property 264 sequences, 1332 calls, 579 fresh questions, 203 exact resumes, 343 answers with no question open, 156 refused.

## 2026-11-08: approval guard F1/F2/F3 fixed (verify-approval-guard findings)

What changed:
- `mcp.rs` keeps one open 2026-07-28 question (`Held`: key, batch step, question text) for `tap`, `type` and `batch`. The key is the tool name and its arguments.
- A retry's answer counts only when its key matches the open question. Any other answer is dismissed, and nothing is tapped (F1).
- The key is compared before the question is taken, so a stray answer leaves another call's question open (F2).
- A decline or a dismissal on a retry now quotes the held question (F3 message gap).
- An `autonomous: true` call ignores a stray answer.
- `tests/guard.rs` now starts its 2026-07-28 client with the Discover handshake, so its `mrtr::` half really runs that protocol (F3).
- The F1/F2 tests and the tap/type retry property no longer carry `#[ignore]`.

Evidence:
- `cargo test -p phonedriver --test guard`: 36 passed, on the real 2026-07-28 handshake. With the Discover handshake and before the fix, 2 failed on the decline text.
- `PROPTEST_CASES=60 cargo test -p phonedriver --test guard_property`: 7 passed, in 243.84 s.
- `cargo test --workspace`: 585 passed, 0 failed.
- `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`: clean.

## AX/DX audit: `doctor --device` probes only that device (2026-11-08)

- Found: `phonedriver --device android:emulator-5558 doctor` deployed the agent on all three emulators of the shared adb server (20.3 s; lines for 5554 and 5556 with "6.1 s with deploy"). `--device` was parsed and dropped for `doctor`, as it was for `mcp` before 2026-11-08. I ran it that way once myself, so for a few seconds an agent started and was released on emulator-5554 and 5556 (afterwards `ps` shows no agent process on either; only the testbed app on 5554). This is the failure the `doctor-probes-every-device` note warns about.

A failing agent line no longer says "fix: Run `phonedriver doctor`" (it is doctor); it points to `daemon.log`. `docs/reference.md` says what doctor does without `--device`.
- Tests first (red: 2 failed, output listed `android:emulator-5554` lines for `--device android:emulator-5556`): `doctor_probes_only_the_device_it_is_given` (full id and bare serial), `doctor_names_a_device_it_was_given_that_adb_does_not_list`, and an added assertion in `doctor_says_which_step_failed_when_the_agent_cannot_start`. After: `cargo test -p phonedriver --test cli`: 18 passed. clippy `--tests` clean.
- Live: `phonedriver --device android:emulator-5558 doctor`: 4 Android lines for 5558 only, no simulator warning, 6.8 s total against 20.3 s.

## AX/DX audit: no device says what to check; two daemons killing each other's agent say so (2026-11-08)

## AX/DX audit: a web view without a title stays in the list, so web checkboxes and web typing work on it (2026-11-08)

The same test gates the paste-to-keys fallback and the late-text wait for web fields. Raw dump (`snapshot --save-fixture`): `{"cls":"android.widget.CheckBox","f":"vVe","text":"Bacon"}`; with a throwaway `getChecked()` printed into the tree, Chrome reports `0` (false) for ticked and unticked boxes, so the tree never carries it.

## AX/DX audit: "nothing changed" no longer says `wait settled`; `--timeout` help says what it times (2026-11-08)

- Found: after a tap on a web checkbox the reply was `ok tapped @e24 "Extra Cheese" at 36,511 · no change after 502ms` / `hint: nothing changed; `phonedriver wait settled` waits for the screen, `phonedriver snapshot` shows it`. The hint is printed only when the screen already settled, so `wait settled` can only return at once, and the reply gives a model no reason to suspect that the tap worked and the list just does not show it. `phonedriver wait --help` described the global `--timeout` as "how long to wait for the screen to settle" though `wait` uses it for its condition.

## AX/DX audit: a failing `tap` on a missing target (2026-11-08)

- Audit: about 3 s before `tap` fails on a missing target. Measured on the fake device through the real daemon: virtual time 0 (`start_paused`), and through the real MCP server and daemon 2.0 ms wall clock (`tap {"target":"Nope"}` -> `no element matches "Nope"`). The daemon path is one tree read, a resolve and the error; it has no wait to shorten. The only 3 s in that path is a device-side tree read: the Android agent's `tree` waits up to `WEBVIEW_WAIT_MS` = 2500 ms for a WebView that has not exposed its content yet. I could not reproduce it (no emulator in this session), so that cause is inferred from the code, not observed.
- Decision: no code change and no tool-description change. The wait is the screen read, shared by `snapshot` and every other call on such a screen, and the tool list is re-read every turn (budget test in `tests/mcp.rs`), so words on `tap` for one device-specific delay cost more than they save. Added `a_tap_on_a_target_that_is_not_there_fails_without_the_daemon_waiting` so nobody adds a retry loop here without an observed failure (passes at once; it pins the measurement, it was not red first).
- Open: re-measure on the emulator where the 3 s was seen, with `PHONEDRIVER_TRACE` phases (`resolve` lap in `act_now`), to confirm it sits in `resolve`'s tree read.

## AX/DX audit: the CLI prints the device line only when it changed (2026-11-08)

- Found: the MCP server sends the `device ...` line once (`Told`), the CLI, a process per call, printed it on every full-screen reply (about 23 tokens each).
- Changed: the daemon keeps, per session name, the last device line a CLI call printed (`told` method, `docs/protocol.md`; no device and no lease needed). `execute` (CLI only; MCP calls `execute_with`) asks `told` after a successful call and drops the line from the text when it is the same; `--json` returns before that, so JSON is byte for byte what it was. A new session, another device, a rotation or a daemon restart prints it again. A `told` failure leaves the line in. `wire::device_line_at` is the one place that finds the line (the MCP `Told` uses it too).

After: `cargo test -p phonedriver --test cli` 20 passed, `--test daemon` told test ok.

## AX/DX audit: a ref this session never saw gets a short error and no screen dump (2026-11-08)

- Found: a new MCP session (`mcp-<pid>`) that used a ref from another session (or an older daemon) got `@e5 is no longer on screen`, which says the screen changed when this session never had that ref; in a `batch` the failure also carried the whole screen (`screen now, against what you last saw:` plus every line).
- Changed: `Error::StaleRef` has `unseen: bool`: true when the session has seen no screen yet or never issued a ref that high (`RefTable::issued`, `Session::unseen`). Message `@e5 belongs to an earlier screen or another session`, hint ``Run `phonedriver snapshot` to read the screen, then use its refs; refs are per session.`` A `batch` that fails on such a ref returns an empty `observation.text`, so `show_batch` prints no dump. A stale ref after the screen changed within the session is unchanged (still "no longer on screen", closest match, and the diff against what the session last saw). SKILL.md says both.
- Tests: core `a_ref_this_session_never_saw_says_so_and_how_to_get_refs` (did not compile before: no `unseen` field); daemon `a_ref_from_another_session_is_named_as_such_and_a_batch_does_not_dump_the_screen` and `a_stale_ref_in_a_batch_still_shows_what_changed_in_this_session` (the guard for the unchanged case). Before the change a probe through the MCP rig gave `@e5 is no longer on screen` (single tap and type); I did not run the batch dump on the old code, it was read from `show_batch`.
- Limit: a ref number another session issued that this session also has on screen resolves to this session's element with that number; nothing can tell the two apart.
- Whole batch: `cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast`: 612 passed, 0 failed, 126 ignored (device tests); `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean; `cargo fmt --check` clean. `mcp.rs` `an_error_is_is_error_with_the_message_and_the_hint` now expects the new unseen-ref hint (that test used a never-issued `@e99`).
## AX/DX audit: one daemon owns a device's agent; a second one fails fast and names the first (2026-11-08)

- Found: starting an agent ran `pkill` on whatever agent was there, so two daemons on one device replaced each other's agent on every call. Reproduced on emulator-5556 (API 26) with a throwaway probe that connected two `AndroidDevice`s at once, 12 rounds: the loser's errors were `the agent was stopped before it was ready` (143), `no hello from the agent`, `adb refused localabstract:...` and `another UiAutomation client`.
- Changed (decision D34, `docs/architecture.md` 6.2, `docs/protocol.md` -32021): the start shell reads `/data/local/tmp/phonedriver-owner` (`pid=<pid> host=<host>`); with an agent running under another name it prints `owned <line>` and exits 98 without killing; the host returns the new `Error::DeviceDriven { id, pid, host }` (`device_driven`, hint "Stop that daemon (`kill <pid>` on <host>) or use another device, then retry."), after asking again 4 times 500 ms apart so an owner that has just exited does not block its successor. A daemon replaces its own agent; no agent running, or no file, behaves as before.
- Tests first, run against the real shell with stub `pgrep`, `pkill` and `app_process` (`agent.rs`): `a_live_agent_another_daemon_owns_is_not_killed` (red with the `exit 98` removed: `left: 0 right: 98`), `this_daemons_own_agent_is_replaced_and_the_owner_file_names_it`, `an_owner_file_with_no_agent_running_is_stale_and_is_taken_over`, `an_agent_with_no_owner_file_is_from_an_older_phonedriver_and_is_replaced`, `an_agent_owned_by_another_daemon_is_an_error_naming_it`; the error table tests in `core/error.rs` cover the new variant (code, name, hint verb). `cargo test -p phonedriver-core -p phonedriver-android`: all ok (151 and 27 passed); `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean.
- Live (emulator-5556, adb over `ssh -L 5043:...:5037 win-ts`, two daemons with separate `PHONEDRIVER_HOME`s, before it no agent from any other session on 5556):
  - `PHONEDRIVER_HOME=A phonedriver --device android:emulator-5556 snapshot` -> screen; `cat /data/local/tmp/phonedriver-owner` -> `pid=14737 host=Hridyas-MacBook-Pro.local`.
  - `PHONEDRIVER_HOME=B phonedriver --device android:emulator-5556 snapshot` -> `error: device android:emulator-5556 is driven by another phonedriver daemon (pid 14737 on Hridyas-MacBook-Pro.local)` / `hint: Stop that daemon (`kill 14737` on Hridyas-MacBook-Pro.local) or use another device, then retry.` in 3.7 s; the agent process (pid 31254) was unchanged before and after, and A's next `snapshot` answered from it.
  - `PHONEDRIVER_HOME=A phonedriver daemon stop`, then B's `snapshot` -> screen in 1.6 s, owner file now `pid=14807`.
- Not covered: two daemons that start in the same few milliseconds can both pass the check (D34 Limits); a daemon from before this change still kills ours.
- Not covered: two daemons that start in the same few milliseconds can both pass the check (D34 Limits); a daemon from before this change still kills ours.

## AX/DX audit: a not-settled launch still carries the screen lines (2026-11-08)

- Found: `launch` on the first call after a daemon restart answered `not settled after 12421ms` and no element lines. A reply of that shape is a full observation whose tree had no elements: the header (`device`, `app ... · not settled after Nms`) is always printed, the lines come from the tree read after the settle, and `report` took that one read as it was (it re-read only when a diff lost lines).
- Changed: `report` re-reads the tree for up to `CONTENT_WAIT` (2 s, every 100 ms) while the rendered screen has no elements, whatever the settle said.
- Test first (`crates/cli/tests/daemon.rs`, fake device with four element-less trees, then the Calculator screen, frames every 50 ms so the settle times out): `a_launch_that_never_settles_still_answers_with_the_screen_lines_once_the_tree_has_them`. Red before: the reply was `{"settled":false,... "text":"device fake:1 ...\napp com.android.calculator2 (Calculator) · keyboard hidden · not settled after 5000ms"}` with no line; green after. `cargo test -p phonedriver --test daemon`: 113 passed.
- Live: not reproduced. Three cold `launch Settings` on emulator-5556 after `daemon stop` and `am force-stop`: `settled in 2940ms`, `3058ms`, `3218ms`, each with its element lines. The 12.4 s case came with two daemons replacing each other's agent (finding 3), which the live runs no longer have; the element-less read is inferred from the shape of the reply, not observed.

## AX/DX audit: a screenshot is never older than the tree it is drawn with, and never comes from a stream nobody has proved alive (2026-11-08)

- Found (reproduced on emulator-5556, API 26, adb over the win-ts tunnel): `AndroidDevice::screenshot` serves `store.latest()` unless the video socket has ended (D10 correction: a quiet stream is not a broken one, so there is no age check, and the API 26 encoder really does go silent on a static screen: 10 frames in the first second, none for the next 7 s, `doctor` says `no frame within 1 s`). A throwaway probe connected an `AndroidDevice`, ran `pkill -f 'dev.phonedriver.agent.[M]ain'` (what a second daemon's start does) and then called `screenshot()` and `tree()` every 250 ms with a 5 s timeout each:

  ```
   5198 ms after kill: shot=ok tree=TIMEOUT
  10454 ms after kill: shot=device android:emulator-5556 disconnected tree=TIMEOUT
  20965 ms after kill: shot=device android:emulator-5556 disconnected tree=TIMEOUT
  21218 ms after kill: shot=device android:emulator-5556 disconnected tree=device android:emulator-5556 disconnected
  ```

  The agent process was gone 234 ms after the kill (`ps`), yet the video socket reported the end after 5 to 10 s and the control socket after about 21 s, so for that long a screenshot returned the dead stream's last frame and tree reads hung. This is the cause I can show: two daemons replacing each other's agent leave each loser holding a stream that looks open. I did not reproduce a frame minutes old with a current tree (a tree that answers means a live agent whose video thread is stuck, or a decoder that is far behind); the barrier below covers both, but I cannot claim it was the cause of that one.
- Changed: the agent has a `video_barrier` control op (`Ops.java`, `Video.java`): it takes every frame already encoded, then writes a barrier packet (flags bit 2, the request's id) into the video stream, and the pump polls every 10 ms instead of 50. `VideoStream::next_barrier` / `wait_barrier` (`stream.rs`, `h264.rs` `WirePacket::Barrier`): the decoder thread reports a barrier only after every packet before it is decoded and published. `AndroidDevice::screenshot` asks for a barrier and waits up to 1.5 s for it (`caught_up`), else `DeviceGone`; the daemon reconnects once and takes the picture from the new agent. `screenshot --marks` reads the tree first and the frame after (it did the reverse, so the frame could be older than the boxes by the whole tree read). Docs: architecture section 8 (screenshot), protocol sections 2 (op, packet).
- Tests first: video crate `a_barrier_passes_only_once_every_packet_before_it_is_decoded_and_shown`, `a_barrier_does_not_pass_for_a_stream_that_has_ended` (red: no `Barrier`, `next_barrier`, `wait_barrier`; green after); android `barrier_tests` (an agent that never answers, an agent that answers while the stream delivers nothing, a barrier that comes back, an agent error); daemon, with the fake device now able to take screenshots (`FakeDevice::with_screenshot`, `fail_screenshots`, `order`): `a_screenshot_with_marks_is_taken_after_the_tree_it_is_marked_with` (red: `left: ["screenshot", "tree"]`), `a_screenshot_whose_stream_was_gone_reconnects_once_and_answers` (red: the DeviceGone reached the caller), `a_screenshot_that_is_gone_twice_is_an_error`.
- Evidence: `cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast`: 611 passed, 0 failed (126 ignored live tests); `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean; `cargo fmt --check` clean. `bash android-agent/build.sh` builds the jar (the one the CLI embeds).
- Live on emulator-5556 (no other agent on it; `PHONEDRIVER_HOME=pdhome-5556`): Settings open, `phonedriver --device android:emulator-5556 screenshot` showed Settings (1:58); then `adb shell "pkill -f 'dev.phonedriver.agent.[M]ain'; input keyevent KEYCODE_HOME"` and at once `screenshot`: 1.9 s, the picture is the home screen with the clock at 1:59, from a new agent. With the barrier the dead agent cannot be mistaken for a live stream; before it the same call returned the Settings frame (probe above).
- Cost: with the daemon built in debug mode and the tunnel's 500 to 800 ms `adb shell true` round trips, daemon-side `screenshot` p50 went from 203 ms (barrier call removed, n=20) to 280 ms (n=20): one control round trip plus the barrier's way back through the stream. Co-located and in a release build that is a local adb round trip and at most 10 ms of pump poll; the 30 ms budget was not re-measured there.
- Not done: the barrier does not prove the encoder is producing frames (an encoder wedged on a changing screen still passes it, because the agent has nothing to write before the barrier); no timestamp check of frame age against the screen. iOS (CoreDevice stream, Simulator polling) is unchanged.

## 2026-11-08: the a11y cache change is held back (branch t3/keep-a11y-cache-web-reads, b0f85d3)

- The change: the agent keeps the accessibility cache between reads, and empties it only after an event or one of our own ops.
- Measured gain, back-to-back reads only: Wikipedia went from 741.6 ms to 254.8 ms p50, and dominos from 302.1 ms to 193.2 ms. A read after an action, which is the usual agent loop, shows no change: Wikipedia 1391 ms before, 1404 ms after.
- Why it is not merged:
  - The committed build never ran the live suites (`e2e`, `actions`, `coverage`, `device_live`, `agent_live`, `tree_cache`), because win-ts stopped answering SSH.
  - A change that raises no event would come back stale.
  - A stale screen is worse than a slow one.
- Next: run those suites, then decide. Before merging, renumber its decision to D35, because D33 and D34 are taken.

## AX/DX audit: the diff says when elements stop or start being under the keyboard; a repeated label is said once (2026-11-08)

- Found (2): `under-keyboard` is printed from the header's keyboard frame, not stored on the element, so `diff` never compared it. After `press back` the reply was `- @eN` (the keyboard) only, and the four lines that had lost the tag were not in it, so the agent called `wait settled` and got the whole screen. Found (3): `~ @e1 button "Go forward" "Go forward" → (none)`: a button with no text of its own takes its label from the first text child and its hint from the element's hint and the other children, which can repeat the label (here the element's own hint and a second child, both "Go forward"), and `diff` compared raw hints although the line never prints a hint equal to its label.
- Changed: `Rendered::under_keyboard(e)` (the old inline test in `line`); `diff` compares it as `Field::Covered`; `text` prints those changes grouped by direction: `~ @e201 @e309 under-keyboard → (none)` and `~ @e1 @e2 (none) → under-keyboard`, one line each however many elements. Keyboard-tag changes are not "the screen moved beyond the field" for the typing check (`moved_beyond`), as before. `prune` leaves out of a merged hint the texts equal to the label or to an earlier hint. `diff` compares the hint as printed (a hint equal to the label is none).
- Tests first: red was `left: "" right: "~ @e1 ... under-keyboard → (none)"` and `Diff([Changed { field: Hint, from: "\"Go forward\"", to: "(none)" }])` for the diff tests, `(Some("Go forward"), Some("Go forward · Go forward"))` for prune. `a_hint_that_only_repeats_the_label_is_not_a_change_when_it_goes`, `elements_that_stop_being_under_the_keyboard_are_one_line_and_so_are_those_that_start` (diff.rs), `texts_that_repeat_in_a_merged_label_are_said_once` (prune.rs), `hiding_the_keyboard_says_which_elements_are_no_longer_under_it` (daemon.rs). `cargo test -p phonedriver-core` 157 passed; `cargo test -p phonedriver` no failures.
- Live (emulator-5554, API 37; testbed Text tab, display shrunk with `wm size 1080x1400` so the keyboard covers fields, reset after):
  - `phonedriver tap @e199` then `phonedriver snapshot --diff`: `+ @e263 keyboard` / `~ @e201 @e309 (none) → under-keyboard`
  - `phonedriver press back`: `ok pressed back · settled in 847ms` / `- @e263` / `~ @e201 @e309 under-keyboard → (none)` (before this change only the `- @e263` line)
- Not live (3): the `Go forward` button came from a Chrome toolbar I could not bring back on this emulator; the unit test rebuilds its shape.
- One commit for both findings: they change the same functions in `diff.rs`.

## AX/DX audit: web radio buttons show `on`/`off` like web checkboxes; a native radio says its state once (2026-11-08)

- Found: on `https://httpbin.org/forms/post` (emulator-5554, Chrome, API 37) `radio "Small"`, `radio "Medium"`, `radio "Large"` printed no state, and `tap "Medium"` answered `no change after 504ms` though the page's screenshot shows the dot. `daemon/checkbox.rs`'s screenshot read ran for `Role::Checkbox` only.
- What Chrome exposes (a throwaway `dbg` field with `AccessibilityNodeInfo.toString()` and `refresh()` for every RadioButton and CheckBox, built into the agent jar, run, and reverted with `git checkout` of `Accessibility.java`): for the selected "Medium" `checkable: false; checked: false; stateDescription: null; contentDescription: null; focused: true`, `refresh()` returned false for `isChecked`/`isCheckable` and null for the state description, and no extras beyond the actions list (ACTION_CLICK, ACTION_CLEAR_FOCUS, ...). The same for the unselected two and for the four checkboxes. So there is nothing in the tree to read; the screenshot read is the only source, as D26 says for checkboxes. No Java change is part of this commit.
- Calibration on the saved screenshot (1 px per pt): the radio is 7x7 pt, the selected one has 15 of 16 pixels in the inner 60% saturated (share 0.94), the unselected two 0 of 20. The same `ticked` test and 20% threshold work, so only the role filter changed.
- Changed: `read_checkboxes` reads `Role::Radio` too; a tap on a web radio waits for the repaint only when it was off (a radio cannot turn off by being tapped, so a tap on the selected one would wait the whole 3 s). The native Settings radio printed `radio "not selected" off` (its `stateDescription` is `not selected`/`selected` and shows as the value); `shown_value` treats `selected` and `not selected` as the state word it repeats, so it prints `radio off` / `radio on`, and the diff for a change is one line instead of two.
- Tests first: `a_web_radio_shows_on_or_off_like_a_web_checkbox_and_a_native_one_shows_its_own_state` (mcp.rs; red: `@e2 radio "Medium"` with no state), `a_radio_whose_state_description_only_says_selected_prints_its_state_once` (render.rs; red with the words removed: `@e1 radio "not selected" off`), and `a_radio_is_ticked_when_its_dot_is_colored_and_not_when_only_its_ring_is_drawn` (checkbox.rs, a characterization of the dot geometry; it passed before the change because `ticked` is role-blind). `cargo test -p phonedriver-core` 158 passed.
- Live (emulator-5554), web radios on httpbin.org/forms/post:
  - `phonedriver snapshot`: `@e18 radio "Small" off` / `@e19 radio "Medium" on focused` / `@e20 radio "Large" off`
  - `phonedriver tap "Large"`: `ok tapped @e20 "Large" at 15,218 · settled in 1575ms` / `~ @e19 radio "Medium" on → off` / `~ @e20 radio "Large" off → on`
  - `phonedriver tap "Large"` again: `no change after 504ms` at once (no 3 s wait)
  - `phonedriver tap "Small"`: `~ @e18 radio "Small" off → on` / `~ @e20 radio "Large" on → off`; `phonedriver tap Bacon`: `~ @e23 checkbox "Bacon" off → on`
- Live, native radios (Settings → Display & touch → Screen timeout): before `@e31 radio "not selected" off` and a tap printed `~ @e62 radio "not selected" → "selected"` plus `~ @e62 radio off → on`; after `@e8 radio off` and `phonedriver tap "30 minutes"`: `~ @e14 radio on → off` / `~ @e20 radio off → on`.
- Seen, not fixed: on this emulator `tap "Stay signed out"` and `tap "Search Settings"` each answered `no change after 2001ms` although the screen had changed a moment later (Chrome first run, Settings search).

## AX/DX audit: a dialog that closes over an unchanged screen is one line (2026-11-08)

- Found: after `tap "Force stop"` on Settings → Apps → Chrome (emulator-5554) the dialog is its own accessibility window (`windows` has only the dialog's), so the app screen leaves the tree. `tap "Cancel"` then brings the app screen back with 100% of the dialog's refs removed and 14 lines added, which `diff` calls a new screen (`> 0.6` changed) and answers with the whole screen. Measured live before the change (both replies as printed, characters): open 366 (dialog only: 4 lines), close **865** (the 12 element lines of App info, the header and the `ok tapped` line). Opening a dialog was already small here because the screen under it leaves the tree; the repeated nav is on the way back.
- Changed: `Session` remembers the screen a new screen replaced (`below`) for as long as the agent is still shown that new screen. `diff_below(prev, now, below)` (core) answers a would-be `Full` with a diff against `below` when at most a quarter of `now`'s lines differ from it, led by a marker; `Session::diff_for_reply` is used by `snapshot --diff` and by every action's reply (`finish`); `batch` and `wait settled` still diff or print without it. The marker line is `back to the screen from before, unchanged` or `back to the screen from before, except:` followed by the usual `-`/`+`/`~` lines. Refs of the screen that came back are the ones it had (the ref table already restores them, test `a_dialog_comes_back_from_a_popup_with_the_refs_it_had`). A genuinely different screen, a first screen, or a screen with more than a quarter of its lines changed is still sent whole, and so is any reply when no screen is remembered below.
- Tests first: `closing_a_dialog_that_replaced_the_screen_says_the_screen_is_back_instead_of_printing_it_again`, `a_screen_that_came_back_with_a_few_changes_lists_them`, `a_different_screen_after_a_dialog_is_new_and_so_is_any_screen_with_nothing_below` (diff.rs; red: `diff_below` did not exist) and `a_dialog_that_closes_is_told_as_the_screen_being_back_not_as_a_new_screen` (daemon.rs, the real daemon with a fake device; red with the memory disabled: `"kind":"full"` and the whole screen). `cargo test --workspace`: 611 passed, 0 failed; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean; `cargo fmt --check` clean.
- Expected reply after (computed from the format, **not run on a device**): `ok tapped @e346 "Cancel" at 236,521 · settled in 501ms` plus `back to the screen from before, unchanged`: 97 characters against 865 (768 saved, 89%), and the model keeps its earlier screen.
- **Live proof not done.** After the last live run, win-ts stopped answering (`ssh: connect to host 100.90.237.82 port 22: Operation timed out`, ping 100% loss, for 5+ minutes), so the tunnel to emulator-5554 is gone and I could not run `tap "Force stop"` / `tap "Cancel"` with the new build. Before that, the emulator's agent had stopped starting (`the agent did not print ready within 3 s`) after Settings raised two ANR dialogs; I tried `adb reboot` on emulator-5554 and the connection dropped at that moment, so the emulator's state is unknown. To prove: `ssh -N -L 5044:127.0.0.1:5037 win-ts`, then with `ADB_SERVER_SOCKET=tcp:127.0.0.1:5044 phonedriver --device android:emulator-5554`: `launch settings`, `tap Apps`, `tap Chrome`, `tap "Force stop"`, `tap "Cancel"`; the last reply should be the two lines above.

## 2026-11-08: a screenshot encodes while the video barrier travels

- Changed: `newest_png` in `crates/android/src/device.rs`.
  - The PNG of the frame on hand is encoded at the same time as the video barrier (`caught_up`) travels.
  - When the barrier passes, the encoded PNG is served if no newer frame arrived. Otherwise the newest frame is encoded and served.
  - A screenshot now costs max(barrier, encode), not barrier + encode.
  - The tree-before-frame order for `--marks` is unchanged.
- Why: the barrier (f7f1324) raised daemon screenshot p50 from 203 to 280 ms over the win-ts tunnel in a debug build. The speed chart's 11.6 ms p50 (docs/bench/speed.md) was measured before the barrier existed.
- Tests: `a_frame_that_arrives_while_the_barrier_travels_is_the_one_served` and `a_barrier_that_fails_fails_the_screenshot`.

- `cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast`: 644 passed, 0 failed. Clippy is clean.
- Not measured: the co-located release screenshot p50. win-ts is offline (Tailscale shows `mmhv` offline). Re-run the screenshot row of `bench/speed` before the chart's 41x is quoted again.

## 2026-11-08: real-phone token fixes (Honor X8, Android 13)

The fixes come from the first run on a physical Android phone, a Honor X8 (TFY-LX2), on the MagicOS launcher, Settings and Google search.
- **Bidi controls are stripped** where `raw.rs` reads each string (`non_empty`): U+200E, U+200F, U+202A to U+202E and U+2066 to U+2069.
  - The launcher's weather widget printed `"‪6degrees celsius‬"`. Its `@e17 text` lines held only marks and came out as empty `text` lines.

- **A hint the label already holds is not printed** (`render::shown_hint`, shared with the diff).
  - The rule: the hint is dropped when the label holds all the hint's words, in order, case and punctuation ignored.
  - Google search rows printed `"Recent search brightness control." "brightness control"` on every row.
  - Values are not dropped: a field's text always prints. Test: `a_field_value_the_label_contains_is_still_printed`.
  - Test: `a_hint_whose_words_the_label_already_holds_is_not_printed`.
- **A list whose collection info says 0 rows prints no count.** Settings printed `@e69 list 0 rows` above 9 rows. Test: `a_list_without_a_row_count_does_not_say_zero_rows`.
- Each test above failed before its change.
- `cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast`: 651 passed, 0 failed. Clippy is clean.
- Open, not reproduced: just after the user unlocked the phone, `launch`, `scroll` and `tap` answered "The device is locked" within 43 to 81 ms. Earlier, while the lock screen was up, a `launch` answered `ok launched Settings` and listed the lock screen. `Keyguard.locked()` calls `IWindowManager.isKeyguardLocked`. Testing it means locking the phone, which only the user can undo, so it waits for the user.

## 2026-11-08: an opened link replies with its page, not Chrome's empty web view

- Seen in agent run t3: `launch Chrome url=en.wikipedia.org` replied after 3,002 ms with only `@e6 group "Web View" focused`, so Sonnet 5.5 had to snapshot again (1,406 characters). It reproduced with `phonedriver open …` at 1,708 ms.
- Changed:
  - `RawTree::web_view_pending` also counts Chrome's empty node named "Web View", so the header says `loading` while it shows.

- Changed: after a settled tap whose frames changed but whose elements did not, the Tap branch in `crates/cli/src/daemon/actor.rs` settles once more. It waits up to `LATE_PAGE_WAIT` (600 ms) for a first change. If one comes, the reply is that screen, and its settle time includes the first wait. If none comes, the first reply stands. A tap that really changes nothing in the tree (a web checkbox's look) now costs 600 ms more.

## 2026-11-09: scroll --until flings toward a name far down an alphabetical list

- Seen in agent run t9: `scroll down --until "YouTube"` in Settings' list of apps took 23 drags. Numbered rows already fling when the target is far away; names in alphabetical order did not.
- Changed: `sorted_distance` in `crates/cli/src/daemon/actor.rs`.
  - It reads the rows directly inside the scrolled element. If there are at least 4, in order, it says whether the name is ahead or behind, and flings when its first letter is 3 or more letters away.
  - The first time the name falls between two rows on screen, or the rows are out of order, the search goes on in the asked direction without steering. A name the list lacks then reaches the end once, instead of going back and forth. A word inside a label ("Music" in "YouTube Music") is still found further on.
- Tests:
  - `scroll_until_a_name_far_down_an_alphabetical_list_flings_and_a_near_one_drags`: a far name flings, a near name drags, and an unsorted list drags.
  - `scroll_until_a_name_missing_from_an_alphabetical_list_goes_on_one_way_to_the_end` runs on a new `AlphaList` fake whose rows move with each gesture. The first version of the change failed it with `scrolled 30 times and "Gmail" is still not on screen`, the back-and-forth also seen on the phone.

## 2026-11-09: iOS keyboard and toolbar: taps no longer land on keys, and bar buttons are not "under the Toolbar"

- Setup: win-pc is offline, the Android phone is unplugged, and the MacBook has no iOS runtime (7.2 GB free). So the daemon runs on the Mac mini's iPhone 17 Simulator (iOS 26.5), and the MacBook's MCP server reaches it through `ssh -L /tmp/pd-mini.sock:…/daemon.sock`. Agent runs are `claude -p`, Sonnet 5.5, on the subscription.
- Seen in agent runs i2 and i2b (Contacts, a new contact with a phone number; 23 and 18 calls):
  - `tap "Insert add phone"` at 52,590 typed "W" into the name field, and `tap "add phone"` typed "T". Neither row was marked `under-keyboard`, because only a fully covered element was. The XCUI keyboard frame (y 583) also starts 17 pt below the glass panel iOS 26 draws, which starts at y 566 (measured on the screenshot).
  - `scroll down` with the keyboard up replied `no element changed`, because the drag started at y 656, on the keys.
  - `tap @e2` on the contact card's back button failed with `@e2 is under the Toolbar`, because iOS 26 puts a top "Toolbar" over the navigation bar.
- Changed:
  - `crates/ios/src/map.rs`: the keyboard window starts `KEYBOARD_GLASS` (18 pt) above the keys and runs to the bottom of the screen.
  - `Rendered::under_keyboard`: an element is under the keyboard when less than 12 pt of it shows above it.
  - `element_at`: a partly covered element is tapped in its visible part. A tap whose point is on the keyboard is refused as `behind_keyboard`.
  - `scroll`: the area stops at the top of the keyboard (`above_keyboard`).
  - `covered_by`: only an element inside a list or a scrollable element can be under a toolbar.
- Tests:
  - `a_row_the_keyboard_half_covers_is_tapped_above_the_keyboard_and_a_sliver_is_under_it`: it failed first, and the refusal part failed again with the old containment check put back.
  - `a_scroll_with_the_keyboard_up_drags_above_the_keyboard`: it failed with the drag from y 656.
  - `the_keyboard_window_covers_the_glass_panel_around_the_keys_down_to_the_screen_edge`.
  - `tapping_a_row_whose_center_is_under_a_floating_toolbar_is_off_screen_with_a_scroll_hint` now also taps a back button under a top Toolbar. It failed with `@e1 is under the Toolbar`. Its covered row now sits in a list, as on the real Settings screen.
- Live check on the Simulator, mini daemon at this build:
  - `tap @e2` on the contact card: `ok tapped @e2 "Contacts"`.
  - In the new-contact form with the keyboard up, both rows are now marked `under-keyboard`, and both taps are refused with `@e18 is under the keyboard` plus the hide-keyboard and scroll hint. Nothing was typed into the name field.
  - The draft was discarded.
- `cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast`: 683 passed, 0 failed. Clippy is clean.

## 2026-11-09: a tap on an element under the keyboard hides the keyboard and then taps it

- Seen in agent run i2c (Contacts, 13 calls, passed): after the keyboard fix, two taps came back `under the keyboard`, then the agent called `press hide-keyboard` and the same tap again. That is two extra calls for one intent.
- Changed: the Tap branch in `crates/cli/src/daemon/actor.rs`. If the target is under the keyboard and the call settles, it hides the keyboard (the device's own way), settles, reads the screen and finds the element again by its ref, because the form moves. ## 2026-11-09: a busy device names the command that frees a CLI session's lease

- Agent run i10 failed at once: my own CLI `snapshot` on the mini held the lease as session `default`, and the agent's `launch` got `DeviceBusy` for 10 minutes. The design is right: an agent must not take a lease over. But the hint only said to wait, so the agent could not tell the user what to do.
- The `DeviceBusy` hint now adds `or ask the user to run \`phonedriver release\`` for `default`, or `PHONEDRIVER_SESSION=<name> phonedriver release` for another CLI session. A lease held by an `mcp-` session (another agent) gets no command. MCP keeps the command as a terminal command (`ask the user to run phonedriver release in a terminal`), not a tool name.
- Tests: `error::tests::a_device_a_cli_session_holds_names_the_command_that_frees_it_and_one_an_agent_holds_does_not` and a case in `mcp::tests::hints_name_tools_and_arguments` (both red before the change).

## 2026-11-09: `scroll --until`, `wait` and `find` take `A|B`

- In agent run i12 (Settings, Airplane Mode and Wi-Fi), the agent wrote `find: "Airplane|Wi-Fi|Wi‑Fi"` and `scroll until: "Airplane|Wi-Fi"`. phonedriver read the `|` literally, so the reply was `no match`, which reads as "not on this screen". (The Simulator really has neither row, so the run's answer was right for the wrong reason.)
- `Session::seek` (used by `scroll --until` and `wait`) and `Session::find` also try each `|`-separated text; the first match on screen wins. A literal match still counts. `sorted_distance` does not fling a sorted list for an alternation. `tap` is unchanged. The `until`, `find` and `wait` docs say `A|B`.
- Tests: `session::tests::a_search_for_either_of_two_texts_stops_at_the_first_on_screen` (red before) and `actor::tests::a_search_for_either_of_two_names_does_not_fling_through_a_sorted_list` (red with the guard removed).

## 2026-11-09: a launch as the first call on a polled device settles at once

- Several runs began with `launched … · settled in 300xms`. Reproduced on the mini: after a daemon restart, `launch Settings` as the first call settled in 3,000 ms. The same launch after a `snapshot` took 510 ms, and a first-call `tap` took 503 ms.
- Cause: on a polled device (iOS Runner tier, no frames), the settle sees a change only by reading the tree and comparing it with the screen last shown. With nothing shown yet, it never read, so a launch waited out its 3 s expect-change window.
- `settle_probing` now takes its first read when nothing has been shown, and counts that read as the change.
- Launch now laps `apps`, `foreground`, `stop`, `launch` and `app_content` in the trace. A warm `launch Calendar --restart` breaks down as: apps 110 ms, stop 100 ms, launch (simctl plus waiting for the foreground) 1,080 ms, app content 630–1,300 ms, settle 260–430 ms.
- Test: `a_launch_that_is_the_first_call_on_a_polled_device_settles_without_waiting_out_its_window` (settle_ms 3000 before the change).
- Live, with a fresh daemon each time: the first `launch Settings` settled in 508 and 405 ms (was 3,000), with a wall time of 4.0 and 3.5 s (was 7.9; the runner connect is about 2.2 s of it).
- `cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast`: 701 passed, 0 failed. Clippy: 0 warnings.

## 2026-11-09: iOS scrolls stop where the finger stops, and play in fewer points

- A Safari `scroll down` took 4.5 s: `fresh` 210 ms, `gesture` 1,130 ms, settle 2,700 ms (the new laps). Screenshots every 380 ms after the gesture showed the page still moving for 2.3 s. The hold before the lift did not stop the fling.
- Experiment on the mini with raw `gesture` paths on the Ada Lovelace article: a still hold drifted 430 and 582 px after the lift; the same hold moving ±0.5 pt drifted 0 px in both trials. XCTest sends no touch-moved event for a point that does not move, so UIKit keeps the last real velocity.
- A second experiment: the same 400 ms path as 17 points took 1,240 ms; as 5 points (one straight move, three wiggled still samples) it took 870–950 ms and scrolled as far (1,380 px against 1,290–1,401 px), with 0 drift.
- `map::gesture_request` (iOS only) drops an interior point on a straight, constant-speed segment, keeps still samples at least 50 ms apart, moves each kept still sample ±0.5 pt in turn, and rounds coordinates to 0.01 pt. Android is unchanged; its stationary samples already read as stopped.
- Test: `map::tests::a_scroll_goes_to_xctest_as_one_straight_move_and_a_still_hold_that_wiggles` (17 points before the change, 5 after).
- Live on the Ada Lovelace article: `scroll down` 4.5 s → 2.0–2.2 s (gesture 1,130 → 830–850 ms, settle 2,700 → 490–620 ms). `pinch out` and `scroll --until "Developer"`/`"General"` in Settings still work.
- `cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast`: 703 passed, 0 failed. Clippy: 0 warnings.

## 2026-11-09: a short Safari page keeps its web view (regression from 1a1867c)

- Cause: on a short page no web view carries `N pages`, so all three matched the new fold. Each was dropped because it held a web view or sat inside one. With no web view left, `in_web_view` was false, and `type` treated the field as native: it skipped focus following and zoom detection.

## 2026-11-09: web checkboxes are read only where they are drawn; Chrome is not pinched

Agent run a5 (Android Chrome, the httpbin pizza form, not submitted) took 16 calls and 52 s, and the final form was right. The `type "Ring twice"` reply, though, said `Medium on → off`, `Large off → on`, `Bacon on → off` and `Mushroom on → off`. The agent spent calls doubting its taps and then checked with a screenshot. This reproduced on every try with the CLI. Chrome zooms in on the small field and the keyboard holds the zoom, so the checkboxes lie past the left edge. `read_checkboxes` then sampled clamped pixels at the edge. Each `type` here also pinched the page back out (the iOS Safari fix), which on Android did not hold (screenshot: still zoomed after `zoomed the page back out`) and cost time.

- `checkbox::readable`: only a box drawn whole on screen and above the keyboard is read from the screenshot. Any other keeps the state the last observation gave its ref. Unit test `only_a_box_wholly_on_screen_and_above_the_keyboard_is_read`.
- `type` does not pinch on an `android:` device. Test `chrome_on_android_zooms_in_on_a_field_and_is_not_pinched_back_out` fails with the gate removed and passes with it. `FakeDevice::with_id` is new for that test. A first try that keyed the pinch on a field at `x = 0` stopped the iOS pinch too (the zoomed Safari field sits at x≈160; only content past the edge reads x = 0), and the iOS screenshot showed the page still zoomed, so I replaced it.
- Live on Android: tap Medium, Bacon and Mushroom, then `type "Ring twice" --into "Delivery instructions:"` gives `ok typed 10 chars into @e27 · verified · settled in 2417ms` with no radio or checkbox lines. A snapshot shows Medium, Bacon and Mushroom on.
- Live on iOS: `type "Grace" --into "Customer name:"` still gives `…, then zoomed the page back out · verified`.
- `cargo test -q --workspace --no-fail-fast`: no failures. Clippy is clean.

## 2026-11-09: Android web pastes stop waiting a full second; a push removes older agent jars

The Android agent restores the clipboard after a paste once the field shows the change, with a 1000 ms limit. In a web page the focused input node is Chrome's page container (`android.widget.FrameLayout`, seen with temporary logging) or a WebView, and its text never changes, so every web paste waited the full 1000 ms. `TextInput.paste` now finds the focused editable node inside a focus node that is not editable itself, once before the paste. It then polls only that node with `refresh()`. Native fields are unchanged.

- Live, on the emulator's httpbin form: the agent waited 95 and 121 ms instead of 1005 ms (logcat). The trace's `text` lap went from 1033–1148 ms to 186–261 ms, and on a Wikipedia article it was 303 ms. Each `type` reply went from about 2.9–3.0 s to 1.5–2.5 s. Every field verified.
- Live suites on `emulator-5554`: `actions` 34 passed, `e2e` 10 passed, `coverage` web tests 5 passed.
- `deploy` now deletes other `phonedriver-agent-*.jar` files after it pushes a new one. A running agent keeps the jar it opened. The emulator held 52 jars from earlier builds. `deploy_pushes_once_with_mode_0444` now plants an older jar and checks that only the pushed one is left: 1 passed live.
- `cargo test -q --workspace --no-fail-fast`: no failures. Clippy is clean.

## 2026-11-09: `find` takes a role word that no label holds

Rerun a5b (the Android form) took 22 calls and 49 s, against 16 calls and 52 s in a5. There were no false checkbox flips, and each `type` settled in 1.1 to 1.7 s instead of about 2.9 s. The agent spent the extra calls checking its work. It wrote `snapshot find "radio|checkbox"` and got `no match`, then scrolled up and left (Chrome stays zoomed after the keyboard hides, now that Android is not pinched). `Session::find` now treats an alternative that no label or hint holds, and that is a role word, as that role. `find "Text"` on a screen with a "Text" tab still matches the label only.

## 2026-11-09: `open` reuses one browser tab

The emulator's Chrome had reached 14, then 19 tabs, one per `open`, because a VIEW intent without a sender opens a new tab. On a person's phone that litters their browser. The agent's VIEW intent now carries `Browser.EXTRA_APPLICATION_ID` `dev.phonedriver`. Chrome then reuses the tab it opened for that id, and leaves the user's tabs alone. By hand with `am start`: three opens with the extra added one tab in total, and an open without it added another.

- Live test `opening_pages_one_after_another_reuses_one_browser_tab` (open example.com, read "See N tabs", open .org and .net, read it again) failed with 17 against 19 before, and passes now. `open_a_deep_link_shows_that_row_and_a_web_link_shows_the_page` passes too, so links into apps still work.

It started with `shell am start … APPLICATION_DETAILS_SETTINGS`, which answered `screen: no change after 252ms`, and the next `snapshot` printed a header with no app and no lines. That read fell between two activities, and the agent fell back to a screenshot. A settled `snapshot` now reads again every `TREE_STEP` for up to `CONTENT_WAIT` (2 s) while no node has a label or actions, as an action's reply already did.

Not changed; it needs a measurement on a phone with a hardware encoder first.

## 2026-11-09: `open` in the browser already in front no longer waits 2.5 s for it to leave

Every `open` took about 3 s to settle on the emulator, even example.com: `settled in 2881ms`, `3043ms`, `3125ms`, `3176ms`. The open path had no laps, so I added `open_url`, `app_content` and `page`. The trace then showed `app_content` at 2560 to 2800 ms on every call. `until_app_content` waits for a window of an app other than the one in front, for up to `LEAVE_WAIT` (2.5 s). When the URL opens in the browser that is already in front, no other app ever comes, so each call waited out the full 2.5 s. The agent already replied with the package that handles the link, and `AndroidDevice` dropped it. `Device::open_url` now returns `Option<AppId>`: Android gives the package, iOS and CoreDevice give `None` and behave as before. `open` no longer waits for the app in front to leave when that app handles the link.

- Test `a_link_opened_in_the_browser_already_in_front_does_not_wait_for_the_browser_to_leave` uses a fake whose URL handler is the app in front. With the old filter it took 2.85 s and failed; with the fix it passes. The fake's page changes after the open, as a real page does, because a page that stays the same makes settle wait for `expect_change` instead.
- Live, in Chrome already in front: `settled in 1839ms`, `1078ms`, `1496ms`, against 2.9 to 3.1 s before. `app_content` is now 0.7 to 1.2 s, which is the page loading. Opening from Settings still takes 3.2 s, because Chrome has to come to the front.

The new function `ios::map::url_handler(url, simulator)` names `com.apple.mobilesafari` for those links on the Simulator and names none otherwise. A physical iPhone may have another default browser, so it keeps the old wait.

## 2026-11-09: a launch on a device with frames needs one read that shows the app

`app_content` took about 1 s on every warm switch between Settings and Clock. A log of each pass through the loop showed why. Each pass costs 200 to 470 ms (a tree read through the tunnel, the screen state, then 100 ms of sleep). The new app was in front by the second read (`back=true` at 873 ms), but the loop waited one more read to see the node count repeat (`stable=true` at 1080 ms). On a device with frames, settle then waits for quiet frames, and `stabilize` reads again until two reads agree. So the extra read only repeated their work. A device with frames now needs one read that shows the app in front. A polled device (iOS) still needs three equal reads.

- Live, three Settings and Clock round trips: `app_content` 804 to 942 ms against about 1050 to 1100, and a whole launch 1366 to 1579 ms against 1504 to 2006. Each reply shows the new app's own elements.
- Daemon tests: 152 passed. Android live: e2e 10 of 10. In the full suites, `actions` passed 34 of 35 and `states` 7 of 8. The two that failed (`a_shown_keyboard_marks…`, `a_changed_wm_size…`) open testbed tabs with `am start` and never reach this code. Each passed when run alone.

## 2026-11-09: a point outside the screen is an error that names the screen in points

In run a14 (Android: Do Not Disturb from quick settings, using the UI only), the agent's first call was `swipe` from `540,5` to `540,1200`. Those are pixels on a 1080-wide display, while the device line says `411x923pt`. phonedriver answered `ok dragged 540,5 to 540,1200`, the panel did not open, and the agent had to find another way. A tap at `540,1200` answered `ok tapped at 540,1200 · no change`. Every point target (`tap`, `swipe` `from`, `drag`) now goes through `resolve_target`, which refuses a point outside the screen with the new error `point_off_screen` (-32024): `540,1200 is outside the screen, which is 411x923 points`. The hint reads `Use points, not pixels: an x,y inside 0,0 to 411,923, or a ref from phonedriver snapshot.` Points on the edge are allowed.

- Daemon test `a_point_outside_the_screen_is_refused_with_the_screen_in_points_and_nothing_is_sent` failed with `tapped at 540,1200` and passes now. It checks the tap and the drag, that no gesture reached the device, and that `360,800` is still accepted. `protocol.md` lists the code. Core tests: 202 passed. Daemon tests: 153 passed. MCP tests: 30 passed.
- Live: `tap 540,1200` gives the error and hint above, and `drag 200,5 200,600` still works.

## 2026-11-09: runs a12 to a14, open findings

So the agent's answer, that it could not find it, was correct. The agent's next scroll then went past Download through Ringtones without seeing them. Four warm repeats each listed all folders, so this was a cold listing still loading after settle. The switch's label keeps the trailing ". " of its content description, and the Do Not Disturb tile repeats its state in its label (`switch "Do Not Disturb. On" on`). This is cosmetic, so I left it. ## 2026-11-09: a screen whose content is still invisible counts as loading

- Seen: after `pm clear com.android.chrome`, `launch Chrome` answered with one line, `@e1 list 2 columns scrollable→`. A snapshot a second later listed the first-run title and both buttons.
- Cause, from a saved tree: the title and buttons were in the tree with flags `ve`, not `vVe`. Chrome fades them in, and Android calls them not visible to the user until then. Before that, Chrome shows a spinner for 1 to 2 s.
- `RawTree::loading` is now also true when the tree holds texts or buttons and none is visible to the user. The launch wait (`until_app_content`) needs one visible text, value or tappable node.
- Tests: `a_launch_waits_while_the_app_has_drawn_nothing_to_read_or_tap` (daemon, failed first with the one-line reply) and `a_screen_whose_texts_and_buttons_are_all_still_invisible_is_loading` (core, written with the code, not seen red).
- `a_launch_that_never_settles_…` now connects before its launch. In paused test time the connect and the launch had the same timestamp, which a real first call never has.
- Live, emulator-5554, three cold launches after `pm clear`: each reply lists "Make Chrome your own", "Add account to device" and "Stay signed out" (`settled in 3318ms`, `3929ms`, `3007ms`). `tap "Stay signed out"` then answered `settled in 290ms · new screen` with the notifications page.
- The earlier `no change after 503ms` on this screen did not come back in two tries. It is not proven fixed.
- `cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast`: no failures.

## 2026-11-09: a list that is still filling is not reported as settled

- Reproduced the Files finding from run a12. After `pm clear` of Files and a stop of its storage providers, `tap "sdk_gphone64_x86_64"` in the roots drawer listed 5 or 6 of the 24 buttons in 3 of 5 runs (`settled in 288ms` to `688ms`). A snapshot 2 s later listed all 24.
- Two causes, from the replies and a trace:
  - The grid said 22 rows, held 6 cards, and had 1,310 px of nothing under them, and two reads 100 ms apart agreed. `RawTree::loading` is now true for a vertical scrollable list that says it has more rows than it shows and has more than twice its last row's height empty below it. A web table (Hacker News, 92 rows, not scrollable itself) is excluded; the committed fixture is in the test.
  - With that rule alone, 1 run in 6 was still short: the trace showed `stabilize: 1245.9` ms, so the tree was still changing when the 1 s limit ended the wait. `stabilize` now waits up to `LOADING_WAIT` while each read holds more nodes than the one before.
- Tests: `a_list_with_more_rows_than_it_shows_and_empty_space_below_them_is_loading` (core) and `a_list_that_keeps_growing_past_a_second_is_read_once_it_stops` (daemon, failed first with 7 of 17 rows).

- `cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast`: one failure, `a_frame_of_412_by_915_takes_under_120_ms_at_the_median` (an OCR time limit), with the Mac at a load average above 70 from other sessions. To rerun when the machine is idle.
- Grid items cut off at the bottom of the screen still print as unlabeled `button` lines. Open.

## 2026-11-09: a web radio that was seen to flip does not wait again for a first change

- Measured on the iOS Simulator (httpbin form in Safari, five radio taps right after `open`): each reply said `settled in 2002ms` to `2006ms`, and the daemon took 3,224 to 5,541 ms per tap. The note from earlier, "about 0.25 s longer", understated it.
- Cause: `await_toggle` reads the tree until the radio flips. The settle that follows then compares against that tree, sees no first change, and waits its whole window: 2 s within 30 s of an `open` or `launch` (`COLD_CHANGE_WAIT`), 500 ms later.
- `await_toggle` now sets `change_seen`, and the settle takes that as its first change.
- Test: `a_web_radio_seen_to_flip_on_a_polled_device_does_not_wait_again_for_a_first_change` (failed first at 600 ms: 100 ms for the flip and the full 500 ms window).
- Live, same five taps: `settled in 250ms` to `257ms`, daemon 1,071 to 1,436 ms per tap. The diffs are the same (`~ @e9 radio "Medium" off → on` and the radio that went off). `tap Bacon` (a switch in Safari) is unchanged at `settled in 480ms`.
- `cargo test -p phonedriver --test daemon`: 156 passed. Clippy is clean.
