## 2026-11-10: the Android agent builds on Windows

CI run 42888938346 was the first to reach Windows. `cargo clippy` failed in `crates/android/build.rs`: on the runner `bash` is WSL's launcher ("Windows Subsystem for Linux has no installed distributions"). Under Git Bash `android-agent/build.sh` failed too: `d8` is `d8.bat`, `javac` wants `;` between source paths, and `aidl.exe` rejects a source file given with forward slashes ("directory ./ is not found in any of the import paths: .\"). The release workflow runs the same script on its Windows runner, so the first release would have failed there.

Changed: `build.sh` picks `d8.bat`, `;` and Windows paths for aidl under MINGW/MSYS/CYGWIN. `ci.yml` builds the jar in a `shell: bash` step and sets `PHONEDRIVER_AGENT_JAR`, as the release setup does.

Proof: on win-pc (Git Bash, build-tools 37.0.0, javac 22) `ANDROID_HOME=/d/Android/Sdk bash android-agent/build.sh` exits 0 and writes a jar with one `classes.dex` (97,052 bytes). On the MacBook the script still exits 0 (96,904 bytes, javac 17). The Windows-built jar has not been run on a device.

## 2026-11-10: the test suite runs on Windows; two OCR tests skip on hosted runners

CI run 42889844605: Windows built the agent jar, then failed to compile `crates/android/tests/build_script.rs` (Unix permissions). macOS failed two OCR tests: Vision on the runner read "b0" as "bo" and took 580 ms a frame against the 120 ms limit.

A first full `cargo test --workspace` on win-pc showed 91 failing tests in 7 targets. Causes: test rigs listened on a file path where Windows needs a named pipe ("not a named pipe path"); four agent tests run stub `sh` scripts; a checkout with CRLF made `skill_prints_skill_md_exactly` fail, and the binary would embed `SKILL.md` with CRLF.

Changed: the rigs in `guard_rig.rs`, `mcp.rs`, `progress.rs`, `dx.rs` and `cli.rs` use a pipe name on Windows, as `daemon.rs` already did. `build_script.rs` and the four stub-script tests are `cfg(unix)`. `.gitattributes` pins LF. The two OCR tests return early when `GITHUB_ACTIONS` is set.

Proof (win-pc, Windows-built jar): `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` finished clean; `cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast`: 48 targets ok, 726 passed, 0 failed, 96 ignored. Observed and not fixed: a test run leaves one `phonedriver.exe` daemon alive on Windows, which blocks the next build until it is stopped.

## 2026-11-10: 0.1.1, the npm launcher unpacks on Windows under Git Bash

`install-check` run 42901155514: four runners ok, `windows-2022` failed: "could not unpack phonedriver-x86_64-pc-windows-msvc.zip with tar: tar: Cannot connect to C: resolve failed". Under Git Bash the first `tar` on PATH is GNU tar, which reads `C:\...` as a remote host. Claude Code on Windows runs through Git Bash, so this would hit its users. Reproduced on win-pc with the published package: `npx -y phonedriver@0.1.0 --version` in Git Bash gives the same error.

Changed: the launcher calls `%SystemRoot%\System32\tar.exe` on Windows (`tarProgram`). Test first: `on Windows the archive is unpacked by the system tar, not the first tar on PATH` failed ("tarProgram is not a function"), then 6 pass. npm versions cannot be replaced, so the version is 0.1.1 everywhere.

Proof on win-pc with the fixed launcher: `node npm/bin/phonedriver.js --version` prints `phonedriver 0.1.0` from Git Bash (download, then cache) and from cmd. On the mini: fmt and clippy clean; `--test cli` 19 pass, 1 fail (`no_device_exits_4…`, the known mini-only failure: the mini has devices).

- `scripts/brand.ts`: new icons (512 and 180 full cut, 32 and 16 micro), lockups, figure files; the icon is on the social images (top right) and the README banner (beside the tagline). Headless Chrome sometimes exits 2 after writing a screenshot ("Teardown watchdog expired"), so `png()` only fails when the file is missing.

## 2026-11-11: first outside score and the fixes from it (D44)

- A separate critic, given only the screenshots and the audit of `b8ecabb`, scored the site Design 7.0, Usability 7.0, Creativity 7.0, Content 8.0 (weighted 7.1). Its top complaints: the inner pages look like another site; a phone on an inner page can reach only Docs and GitHub; no page transition; the reduced-motion hero is a weak frame; row and tab hovers barely change; the blog title breaks at a hyphen.
- Built: `components/PageTop.tsx` and the `data-intro` mode in `lib/journey-engine.ts` (docs, benchmarks, compare, blog, 404 each open as one stop of the route); a phone menu in `components/Header.tsx` (Popover API); a view transition on `<main>` in `app/layout.tsx`; `data-weave="thin"` on two home stops; row hover arrows, chip links on the benchmarks page, scroll shadows on wide tables; `--say` for the spoken line's colour.
- Found while checking it: (1) `lib/run.json` lacked `reached`, `asked` and `held`, which the hero engine reads, so the hero never played its held-for-approval moment and the reduced-motion frame was the lock screen at 0:00. Restored; this changes what the hero does at its end, and the user has not seen it yet. (2) After a client-side navigation Next scrolled the new page 460 to 610px down. It calls `scrollIntoView` on each child of the page, and `scroll-behavior: smooth` on `<html>` let the first call win. `data-scroll-behavior="smooth"` on `<html>` fixes it. (3) On a throttled phone the inner pages shifted 39px when the script arrived (CLS 0.035, and 0.065 on the 404 with a font-swap rewrap of the lede). The plain spoken line is now out of the flow and the page-top lede is balanced. (4) The awwwards `serve.mjs` has no HTTP Range support, so video seeks fail under it; `pd-qa/rs.mjs` on the Mac mini serves `out/` with ranges.
- Evidence, Mac mini, built site (`pd-qa/shots/summary.json`): all six page types `overflow false, smallTargets 0, hiddenTextReduced 0, axeSerious 0, noVisibleFocus 0, noHoverChange 0, desktopFps 60, mobileFps 60, mobileCls 0`; phone LCP at 4x CPU and slow 4G: home 1536 ms, docs 1008, benchmarks 964, compare 984, blog 1000, 404 924; desktop LCP home 168 ms. Navigation probe (`pd-qa/nav.cjs`), docs→blog, blog→docs, home→benchmarks, benchmarks→compare: `scrollY` 0 at every sample from 60 ms to 2.7 s (before the fix: 478, 459, 547, 610). `document.startViewTransition` called once per navigation. The phone menu is closed after a tap on one of its links. `pnpm typecheck` clean, `pnpm check` `problems 0`.
- The 404 run lists one failed request, `favicon.svg net::ERR_ABORTED`: the script navigated away while it was loading. The audit's `2 FAIL` are still the two `example.com` strings.
- Screens looked at: the five page tops at 1440 dark, benchmarks and docs at 1440 light, all five at 390 with the open menu; the page tops with no JavaScript and with reduced motion; a slowed transition into the home page; the eight home stops settled in dark and light; the row and chip hover pairs; the benchmarks page at 390 down to the footer; the reduced-motion hero (now the held frame with the glove on Buy with GPay).

- Evidence, Mac mini, built site (`pd-qa/shots/summary.json`), all six page types: `errors 0, overflow false, smallTargets 0, hiddenTextReduced 0, axeSerious 0, noVisibleFocus 0, noHoverChange 0, desktopFps 60, mobileFps 60, mobileCls 0`; phone LCP home 1560 ms, docs 1040, benchmarks 1004, compare 1028, blog 1028, 404 956; desktop LCP home 196 ms. - Text under 12.5px, measured on home, benchmarks and an install page at 1440 and 390 (`pd-qa/small.cjs`): only the hero log (11.5px, and its note at 10.8px), the snapshot box label (11px) and 12px controls.

## 2026-11-11: site frame cost, and the 11 November speed numbers on the site

- Reported: the page felt slow on the MacBook (load average 31 to 33 from other sessions at the time).
- Found, with a Chrome profile on the Mac mini at 1728x1117, device scale 2 (`perf.cjs`, `trace.cjs` in the session scratchpad): the page held 60 fps on an idle M4 but ran a style pass and a forced layout on every frame at rest. Causes: (1) `pk-a-y`, `pk-b-y`, `pk-a-x`, `pk-b-x` animated `top` and `left` forever, also off screen; (2) the journey tick called `getBoundingClientRect` on all eight stops every frame; (3) `paint` called `getPointAtLength` after writing; (4) the hero wrote `hidden` on every log row and `aria-valuetext` every frame and read `scrollY` after its writes; (5) each token dot ran its own scale transition.
- Changed: packets animate `translate` in container units and pause when their stop is not near; stops cache their document offset at layout and the tick reads a scroll offset kept from scroll events (`lib/frame.ts`); the packet's fall is a table built at layout; a body resize that moves no landing target no longer rebuilds the ribbons; hero row and slider writes happen on change; dots appear without a transition; the ribbon draws at most 60 times a second when the page is still.
- Proof (same probe, before then after): main-thread work per second at rest below the hero 135 to 179 ms, then 39 to 94 ms; layouts per second 60, then 1; a full scroll 221 ms, then 167 ms. At 6x CPU throttle a full scroll had 139 frames over 25 ms of 338 (p95 51 ms), then 108 of 358 (p95 34.6 ms, 4 over 50 ms against 30).
- Not fixed: under 6x throttle a first scroll still drops frames, since the typed line, the dot count and the footer mark (about 570 circles, each with its own transition) repaint on the main thread. Not measured on the MacBook itself or on a 120 Hz display.
- Numbers: merged main (`54067dc`). Site decisions D33 to D41 are now D36 to D44, because main had taken D33 to D35. Hand-written figures moved to the 11 November run with 0.1.1: `site/content/compare/{adb,phone-harness,mobile-mcp}.md`, the speed post (renamed to `how-phonedriver-reads-a-screen-170x-faster-than-adb`), the FAQ in `lib/data.ts`, the benchmarks page notes (the tap note is now computed), the data downloads (`speed-2026-11-11`), banner, social preview and og image (`pnpm brand` on the mini: "170× FASTER THAN ADB").
- Checked: `pnpm build` and `pnpm check` on the mini, problems 0; shoot run on six page types: console errors 0, 60 fps, CLS 0, no serious axe violations. Screens looked at: speed stop (170x, 32x, 23x, 4.8x), tokens stop mid-count, how it works at 1440 and 390 with a packet on each track, compare/adb, blog index.

## 2026-11-11: `then` on tap and type, a path of taps in one call

- Asked by the user: phone-harness used fewer tokens than phonedriver on two of the nine jobs (clock-settings, messages-pin-archive) because its agent writes multi-step scripts. Close the gap, then, after the measurements below: keep the working multi-step form, put it in the skill, and do not make the measured setup worse.
- Found: Sonnet never called `batch` in the 10 November runs. clock-settings took 13 single calls against 9 scripts for phone-harness, and each turn re-reads about 6,500 tokens.
- Changed: `tap` and `type` take `then`, a list of texts to tap next on the screens that follow. The call runs as a batch, stops at the first text that is missing and returns that screen. It runs on both protocol paths (`call_guarded` for 2026-07-28 clients; the first version missed that path and Claude Code 2.1.322 silently ran only the first tap). `then` is refused with `secret`. It is hidden from the lean tool schema, so `tools/list` is unchanged; a list sent as a JSON string or one bare text is accepted, because a model that learns `then` from the skill guesses its type. `SKILL.md` and one README sentence teach it. The tool descriptions and server instructions are as before.
- Tests (win-pc, `cargo test -p phonedriver --lib --test mcp --test guard --test cli`): lib 82, cli 19, guard 38, mcp 35, all pass; each new test failed first. `cargo clippy -p phonedriver --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean.
- Measured (win-pc, Claude Code 2.1.322, Sonnet 5.5, tablet emulator, clock-settings): with the chaining hint in the benchmark's own system prompt, 1 run, 9 calls and 67,057 tokens (phone-harness 79,931 on 10 November). With the hint only in the server instructions or the `tap` description, 7 runs, 11 to 13 calls and 85,256 to 104,450 tokens: the model chained once per run. Before the change, 87,620 and 88,214. So wording in the MCP server does not make Sonnet chain, and no benchmark number changes. The published comparison stands: phone-harness is cheaper on those two jobs.
- Live check of the final binary with the skill's paragraph as the system prompt: `tap {"target":"@e4","then":"[\"Settings\"]"}` returned `ok 2 steps`; the run passed with 77,683 tokens.
- Not done: no rerun of the nine jobs (the tool list and instructions are unchanged). The tablet emulator on win-pc stopped during a build and was restarted with `-gpu swangle_indirect -no-snapshot`; start it with `-gpu host` from a desktop session before the next benchmark. The Mac mini's disk was full (205 MB free), so builds ran on win-pc.

- Asked by the user: make phonedriver a Claude Code plugin, and say whether Codex and Cursor have the same thing.
- Changed: `packaging/plugin/` (manifest, `.mcp.json` that starts `npx -y phonedriver mcp`, a copy of `SKILL.md`, the icon) and `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json` at the root, which makes the repository its own marketplace. Install lines in the README, `site/content/docs/install.md` and `site/content/docs/claude-code.md`. D45 and one sentence in architecture 10.3. CI runs the new test.
- Test first: `node --test packaging/test/plugin.test.js` failed with no plugin folder and now passes, 5 of 5 (skill identical to `SKILL.md`, version equal to `Cargo.toml`, the MCP file starts the launcher, nothing extra in the folder, the marketplace points at the folder). A release must bump `packaging/plugin/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` with the other versions; this test fails until it does.
- Claude Code 2.1.322: `claude plugin validate . --strict` and `claude plugin validate packaging/plugin --strict` print `Validation passed`. Installed from the local marketplace into an empty config: 1 skill and 1 MCP server. `claude --plugin-dir packaging/plugin -p` lists the server `plugin:phonedriver:phonedriver` as connected with 14 tools and the skill `phonedriver:phonedriver`.
- Codex 0.167.0, in an empty `CODEX_HOME`, with only the files above: `codex plugin marketplace add <repo>`, `codex plugin add phonedriver@phonedriver`, then `codex plugin list` says `installed, enabled 0.1.2`, `codex mcp list` shows `phonedriver npx -y phonedriver mcp`, and `codex debug prompt-input` contains the skill `phonedriver:phonedriver`. A root `plugin.json`, `mcp.json` and `.agents/plugins/marketplace.json` were written first and removed: Codex needed none of them.
- Live, through the plugin (`claude --plugin-dir packaging/plugin -p`, Sonnet 5.5, the Android 17 emulator on win-pc over the adb tunnel): asked to turn dark mode off, open Settings and name its first three entries. `mcp__plugin_phonedriver_phonedriver__shell {"command":"cmd uimode night no"}` replied `exit 0 · 121ms | Night mode: no`, `launch {"app":"Settings"}` replied `ok launched Settings`, and the answer was Google, Network & internet, Connected devices, which is the list in the last tool reply. Checked from outside with adb: `Night mode: no` (it was `yes`) and `mCurrentFocus=... com.android.settings/com.android.settings.Settings`. 14 turns: the emulator runs on software graphics over ssh and Settings sat on its splash screen for some seconds, so three snapshots came back empty before the list did.
- Two earlier attempts on the same emulator failed for reasons that are not the plugin. On the first boot (298 s, "Application Not Responding: com.android.systemui") every call returned `the agent exited with code 1 before it was ready`, and `phonedriver snapshot` from the command line returned the same error. On the second, my own command-line `snapshot` held the device lease and the agent was refused with `device android:emulator-5554 is leased by session default`. Dark mode was put back and the emulator shut down.
- The skill: in five runs of a settings job the model called `Skill {"skill":"phonedriver:phonedriver"}` by itself once, as its first step; in the other four it went straight to `shell`, which the server's own instructions already teach. Not run: a Cursor session (no manifest for Cursor was added), the site build (two Markdown pages changed; the Mac mini has 115 MB free), and the `hridaya423/phonedriver` form of the install commands, which works only after this commit is pushed.

## 2026-11-12: a physical iPhone without the cable (D47)

- Asked by the user: drive a real iPhone over Wi-Fi, tested on a real phone. The user plugged in an iPhone 13 Pro Max (iOS 27.0.1), turned on Developer Mode, and approved registering it with the signing team.
- Changed: `crates/ios/src/lan.rs` (new: the remembered udids, the Bonjour browse, the `authTag` match), `transport.rs` (`Attached` with a `Link`, a direct `TcpProvider` for the network, `allow_network`), `device.rs` (`Host::Network`), `setup.rs` and `map.rs` (`devicectl` for the app list and the setup state over the network; the disk image mount fixed for Xcode 27), `discovery.rs` (no tunnel over the network), `crates/cli/src/ios_wifi.rs` (new: `phonedriver ios wifi on|off`), `daemon/ios.rs` (the browse, replaced when a cable comes off), `doctor.rs` (says `over USB` or `over Wi-Fi`). Docs: D47, architecture 7.1 and 6, `docs/reference.md`, the site's iPhone and no-telemetry pages, the changelog. New dependency: `mdns-sd` 0.21.7.
- The first attempt read Wi-Fi devices from usbmuxd's listing. It failed on the phone: after a restart on the cable usbmuxd never listed the phone over Wi-Fi again. The second attempt connects to the phone's lockdown port directly. Lockdown services close over that link, so the runner tier is the only one there. Both findings are in D47.
- Unit tests, on the Mac mini (`cargo test`, blaze is not installed there): `phonedriver-ios --lib` 60 passed; `phonedriver --lib` 90 passed; `--test guard` 38 passed; `--test network` 3 passed. `cargo clippy --all-targets` on both crates prints nothing. The tests for the new code were written with it, not before it.
- Live, cable off, release build on the MacBook, phone on the same Wi-Fi: `phonedriver devices` lists the phone as `ios:<udid>  <name>  27.0.1  runner` in 0.4 s. `phonedriver doctor`: `paired ... tier runner, over Wi-Fi`, `developer mode on`, `developer disk image mounted`, `runner signed`. `launch dev.phonedriver.testbed` from a stopped daemon: `ok launched Testbed`, 8.2 s.

I looked at the screenshot taken over Wi-Fi: the Text tab with "no cable 42" in the first field and the keyboard up. `apps` lists 131 apps.

- The cable pulled in the middle of a session, after the fix in D47: `tap "B9"` 6.95 s, `"B14"` 1.04 s, `"B21"` 1.18 s, all three in the testbed's log. Before the fix the same pull gave `no device` for up to 45 s.

Not rerun on the phone, which no longer answers over Wi-Fi.

## 2026-11-12: the signing team is found, not asked for (D49)

- Asked by the user: smoothness before performance. The team ID was the one step of the iPhone setup with no command, and the hint for it was wrong: it pointed at the ID in parentheses that `security find-identity` prints, which on the MacBook is the developer's ID and not the team.
- `crates/ios/src/team.rs`: `signing_team` reads `PHONEDRIVER_TEAM`, else the organizational unit of the valid Apple Development certificate. The daemon, `ios register`, `ios setup` and `doctor` use it. Several teams are a new `UserAction::ChooseSigningTeam` that lists them (unit test only).
- MacBook, `PHONEDRIVER_TEAM` unset, one Apple Development certificate: `phonedriver ios setup` printed `ok    signing team <team id>`, then `need  a device: plug in an iPhone or iPad, unlock it and tap Trust, then run this again`, exit 1.
- Mac mini, no certificate (`0 valid identities found`): the same command printed `need  signing team: ... This Mac has no valid Apple Development certificate, and PHONEDRIVER_TEAM is not set. Open Xcode > Settings > Accounts, ...`, exit 1.
- Mac mini: `cargo fmt --all --check` and `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets` print nothing. `cargo test -p phonedriver-core`: 208 passed. `cargo test -p phonedriver-ios team::`: 4 passed. `cargo test -p phonedriver --no-fail-fast`: all pass except `cli::no_device_exits_4...` (booted simulator) and four `guard_property` cases ("Too many open files" in the rig), the two mini-only failures noted above.
- Not run: a build and a tap on an iPhone with the detected team. No iPhone was reachable. The team it finds on the MacBook is the one that signed the runner in the Wi-Fi run above.
- `ios setup` also prints a next step when everything is ready, and a tip for `ios wifi on` for a cabled device. Those lines need a device and were not seen yet.
- `untether` on the HONOR X8 failed in 2 of 11 fresh runs of the build before this change: once `failed to connect to '<ip>:5555': Connection refused`, once `connection error: adb sent a bad length [23, 3, 3, 0]`. Both came while adb on the phone restarted; the same command run again connected. `untether` now asks again for up to 6 s on those two answers. After: 10 runs, each from a phone put back to USB mode with `adb usb`, printed `connected:` 10 of 10 in 1.4 to 2.5 s. The phone was left in USB mode.
- `doctor` checked a phone once for each of its connections. On the HONOR X8 with the cable and Wireless debugging both on it printed two sets of four checks and took 10.6 s. With no device named it now folds the connections as `devices` does: one set of checks, 6.5 s. `doctor --device android:<the Wi-Fi name>` still checks that connection.

## 2026-11-12: MCP annotations, and `then` where an agent can see it

- Asked by the user: tool descriptions that follow the standards. The descriptions were already cut to a measured budget; two things were missing.
- Every tool now carries MCP annotations. `snapshot`, `screenshot` and `wait` are read-only, so a client can run them without asking. Test `every_tool_says_whether_it_only_reads_and_the_three_that_do_are_marked`.
- `then` was hidden from `tools/list`, so only an agent that had loaded the skill could send a path of taps. It is in the `tap` and `type` schemas and named in the server instructions. What a model reads of `tools/list` (name, description, schema) is under 4,000 characters; the budget test now counts that, because clients do not pass annotations to the model.
- Mac mini: `cargo test -p phonedriver --test mcp --test dx`: 36 and 6 passed.
- One agent run (`claude -p`, sonnet, subscription, MCP tools only, no skill) on the Pixel API 37 emulator: it turned Dark theme on through Settings in 13 turns and 112 s, `cmd uimode night` then printed `Night mode: yes`. It did not use `then`. The emulator showed "System UI isn't responding" and "Settings isn't responding" during the run, so the run does not show whether agents take up `then` from the schema.
- Found in that run: `phonedriver release` with two devices connected fails with "2 devices are connected; pick one", and a lease held by a terminal command blocked the agent. Not fixed in this commit.
- `release` with no device named now gives up every device the session holds (`release_everywhere`, which the MCP disconnect already used). Before, with the HONOR X8 and the emulator both connected: `error: 2 devices are connected; pick one`. After, same two devices, the emulator leased by `default`: `released`, and `devices` shows `-` under LEASE for both. Test `release_with_no_device_named_needs_no_choice_between_two_devices`.

## 2026-11-12: commands over a slow link (D50)

- Asked by the user: make Wi-Fi commands fast and test at 3G. HONOR X8, read-only commands (the phone was locked): snapshot 96 to 132 ms and screenshot 16 to 31 ms over Wi-Fi (port 5555), against 62 to 144 ms and 11 to 49 ms over USB earlier today. Wi-Fi costs nothing measurable.
- 3G is a TCP proxy that delays each direction 100 ms and limits it to 780 kbit/s down, 330 up. On the HONOR through it: snapshot 534 to 715 ms, 23 KB down for a lock screen whose text is 548 bytes; waking the screen sent 354 KB of video.
- Taps were measured on the Pixel API 37 emulator on win-pc (its adb port through ssh, then the same proxy). The table and the change are in D50: the stream is 400 kbit/s at 10 frames a second when the agent's hello takes 120 ms or more.
- Mac mini: `cargo clippy -p phonedriver-android -p phonedriver --all-targets` prints nothing; `cargo test -p phonedriver-android`: 37, 13, 2 and 4 passed, the live tests ignored.
- Left for later, in order of what they would save on 3G: fewer round trips in a tap (the power-state read is reused for only 1 s, so an agent pays it on every tap), and compressing the tree on the wire (23 KB a snapshot is 240 ms of a 780 kbit/s link).
- Seen once and not reproduced: `type` took 3.1 s on the direct link in the first run after the emulator restarted (`text` phase 3,098 ms); the next run took 619 ms.
- Deflated control frames (second half of D50): the agent compresses frames of 1 KB or more, the host inflates them (`a_deflated_frame_reads_as_the_same_json`). Same emulator and proxy: snapshot 332 to 276 ms, type 1,995 to 1,712 ms, tap 2,237 to 2,174 ms, 0.78 MB against 1.40 MB for the run. Direct link: snapshot 36 ms, tap 569 ms. HONOR X8 over USB: snapshot 92 to 123 ms, screenshot 3 to 9 ms. `cargo test -p phonedriver-android` on the mini: 38, 13, 2 passed.
- Not done: reusing the power-state read for longer than 1 s would save one round trip a tap. It is safe only if a screen that turns off always sends a window or display event, and the one phone here is locked, so that could not be checked on a phone.

## 2026-11-14: Android 12 reads the screen again (issue #7)

- Reported on GitHub (#7): on a Huawei TAS-AL00, Android 12 (API 31), every command that reads the screen failed with `NoSuchMethodException: android.view.accessibility.AccessibilityInteractionClient.clearCache [int]`. `6985be2` had sent API 31 to 33 to `clearCache(int)`, which exists on API 33 (the HONOR) but not on API 31.
- `Accessibility.java`: below API 34 the agent uses `clearCache(int)` when the class has it and `clearCache()` when it does not, instead of choosing by API level. `docs/architecture.md` 6.2 says so.
- New on win-pc: an API 31 emulator, `Pixel_API_31` (google_apis x86_64).
- Before, main's agent jar on `Pixel_API_31`: `accessibility_tree_of_the_taps_tab_has_24_buttons` failed with `{"error":"java.lang.NoSuchMethodException: android.view.accessibility.AccessibilityInteractionClient.clearCache [int]","id":1,"ok":false}`.
- After, `phonedriver` built on win-pc with the fixed jar, on `Pixel_API_31`: `launch Settings` and `snapshot` print the tree; `tap "Battery"` answers `new screen` and the next snapshot shows the Battery page; tapping the Battery percentage switch reads `off → on`, the next snapshot `switch on`, tapping again `switch off` (left as it was); `doctor` is all `ok`, including `uiautomation ... connected, the screen tree reads`.
- Not resolved: the live suite on win-pc hung on its first request with the fixed jar (the agent was idle in its read loop; a thread dump showed no request in flight). The harness had never run on a Windows host (its `gradlew` call needed `gradlew.bat`, patched locally and not committed), so I could not tell a harness problem from an agent one there; the CLI runs above went through the same agent. API 26 and 37 were not run again: API 37 takes the unchanged `UiAutomation.clearCache()` branch, and API 26 reaches the same `clearCache()` call as before.

## 2026-11-15: platform probes on the API 37 emulator (research, no code changed)

- Asked: what Android 17 offers an agent beyond what phonedriver uses. Probed `emulator-5554` on win-pc over adb while no phonedriver agent was running on it. No phonedriver binary was involved, so none of this is proven through our own agent yet.
- App functions answer the shell user. `cmd app_function list-app-functions` listed 58 functions in five system packages (`com.android.settings`, `com.google.android.gms`, `com.google.android.apps.wellbeing`, `com.google.android.permissioncontroller`, `com.google.android.apps.nexuslauncher`). `cmd app_function execute-app-function --package com.android.settings --function getBatteryDeviceState --parameters '{}' --brief-yaml` returned the Battery Saver state as YAML.
- Computer Control is not on this image: `pm list permissions | grep -i comput` shows no `ACCESS_COMPUTER_CONTROL`. `cmd virtualdevice` offers `create-device` and `create-display`, both only with an insecure keyguard.
- Several displays at once: with `settings put global overlay_display_devices "720x1280/240;720x1280/240"`, `am start --display 14 -a android.settings.SETTINGS` and `am start --display 15 -a android.intent.action.DIAL`, `dumpsys activity activities` showed three `topResumedActivity` lines: Messages on display 0, Settings on 14, the dialer on 15. `input -d 14 tap 360 200` gave `mCurrentFocus` to Settings on 14. Window focus moved between displays (display 0 read `mCurrentFocus=null` meanwhile); these overlay displays have no `OWN_FOCUS`, which D32's display asks for from API 34.
- A profile on a second display: `pm create-user --profileOf 0 --managed pdprobe` printed `Success: created user id 10`; after `am start-user 10`, `am start --user 10 --display 15 -a android.settings.SETTINGS` resumed `u10 com.android.settings` on display 15 beside `u0` Settings on 14 and `u0` Messages on 0. The profile listed 211 packages against 268 and had `/data/user/10`. A userdebug image; a production phone is untested.
- Put back: `pm remove-user 10` (`Success: removed user`), `settings delete global overlay_display_devices` (reads `null`), one user listed, Messages in front as before, the probe script removed from `/data/local/tmp`.
- Not done: the same through phonedriver's own hidden display, a physical phone, and the report that Android 17 stops a background display when the screen is off.

## 2026-11-15: the third action in a row that changes nothing says so

- Why: run i18 (architecture 7.3). After a launch that changed nothing, an agent tapped the same icon again and again; every reply said only `no change`.
- `Session` keeps the summary of the last action that settled with no change and its count (`idle`). `Actor::act` raises it when the next action has the same summary, and starts again on a change or a different action. From the third time `ActionResult` carries `unchanged_repeats`, and `show_action` prints `hint: this action has changed nothing 3 times in a row, so repeating it will not help: ...` in place of the usual no-change hint. Protocol section 1 and architecture 4.6 say so.
- Red first, on the Mac mini (`PHONEDRIVER_AGENT_JAR` set to a jar built on the MacBook, since the mini has no build-tools 37): `cargo test --locked -p phonedriver --test daemon identical_action` failed at `daemon.rs:673` (no field), `--test daemon unchanged_repeats` failed 1 of 2, and `--lib stop_repeating` failed with the old hint on the left.
- Green: the same three commands print `1 passed`, `2 passed`, `1 passed`. Then `cargo test --locked --no-fail-fast -p phonedriver --test daemon --test mcp --test guard --test hidden_display --test shell`: 165, 38, 6, 36 and 7 passed, 0 failed. `--lib`: 94 passed. `cargo clippy --locked --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` and `cargo fmt --all -- --check` are clean.
- `--test cli`: 19 passed, 1 failed, `no_device_exits_4_with_the_message_then_the_hint`, which fails on the mini whenever a simulator is booted (as on 13 November).
- Not done: no live run on a device, and no agent run that shows the hint stops the repeats.

## 2026-11-15: `apps grant` and `apps revoke`, and a grant that changed nothing is an error

- Why: the user asked for permission grants as a first-class tool. `launch --grant` could only grant, and only on the way into the app; testing the denied path or the first-run prompt needs a permission taken away between runs. D23 had rejected a `grant` on `apps`; it now records the change and why.
- `apps grant|revoke <app> <permission>...` (daemon method `app`, MCP `apps` with `permissions`). `Device::revoke` is new: `pm revoke` on Android, `simctl privacy revoke` on the Simulator. Same names as `launch --grant`, and the same refusal on a physical device for an app phonedriver did not install.
- Red first on the Mac mini: `cargo test --locked -p phonedriver --test daemon apps_grant` failed 3 of 3 with `the app action is install, stop, clear or uninstall`. Green: 3 passed, and `--test mcp apps_grant` 1 passed.
- Live on the API 37 emulator (win-pc, through the mini), state read from `dumpsys package dev.phonedriver.testbed` after each call: `apps grant dev.phonedriver.testbed read_contacts notifications` printed `ok granted read_contacts, notifications to phonedriver testbed` and both lines read `granted=true`; `apps revoke ... read_contacts` printed `ok revoked read_contacts from phonedriver testbed` and `READ_CONTACTS: granted=false`.
- Found by that run: `apps grant ... camera` printed `ok granted camera` for an app whose manifest has no camera permission. `pm grant` exits 0 and prints nothing in that case on API 37, so `launch --grant camera` had the same false success. Unit tests in `crates/android/src/device.rs` (`permission_tests`) failed 2 of 3 against a stub, then passed: after `pm grant` or `pm revoke` the device reads the `granted=` lines of `dumpsys package` and fails when the state is not what was asked. Live after restarting the daemon: `apps grant ... camera` and `launch ... --grant camera` both exit 1 with `` `pm grant dev.phonedriver.testbed android.permission.CAMERA` changed nothing: the app's manifest does not request that permission ``.
- Whole run: `cargo test --locked --no-fail-fast -p phonedriver --test daemon --test mcp --test cli --test shell`: 168, 37 and 7 passed; `cli` 18 passed, 2 failed: `no_device_exits_4_with_the_message_then_the_hint` (fails on the mini whenever a simulator is booted) and `doctor_says_which_step_failed_when_the_agent_cannot_start`, which passed when run alone straight after. `cargo clippy --locked --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` and `cargo fmt --all -- --check` are clean.
- Not done: `simctl privacy revoke` was not run through phonedriver (the mini has 2.4 GB free and a Simulator session builds the runner); `xcrun simctl privacy` lists `revoke` beside `grant`. The failure above is reported as `connection error` with the doctor hint, like every other `pm` failure; it deserves its own error. No Android level other than 37 was tried. `CHANGELOG.md` is untouched until a release.

(it is 5)`), Battery Saver while plugged in (`Preference not enabled`), the hotspot name (`read-only`), key `dark` (`no setting has the key "dark"; closest: dark_ui_mode/dark_ui_activated, ...`). A read took 0.48 s.

## 2026-11-15: `record`: a video of the screen, with the actions done during it (D59)

- Why: the user's list, "screen recording and logs"; this is the recording half (plan Task 5).
- Built: `crates/video/src/record.rs`, an MP4 writer for one H.264 track (337 lines, no new dependency; `tempfile` is a dev-dependency of the video crate now); a tap in `VideoStream`'s reader that keeps the parameter sets and every unit since the last keyframe, so a recording can start at any moment; `Device::record_start` and `record_stop` (Android only; the default says the device has no video stream); daemon method `record`; CLI `record start [-o file] | stop`; in MCP the `record` argument of `screenshot` (no new tool: the list went from 4,815 to 4,970 characters). While a recording runs, each successful action adds `{"t_ms", "summary"}` to `<file>.steps.jsonl`.
- Tests. The writer: `crates/video/tests/record.rs` parses the file back box by box and decodes its samples with openh264 to the fixture's 59 frames (`2 passed` once the module existed; it could not run red before). The tap: a recording started mid-stream and one whose stream ends (`4 passed`). The daemon: `crates/cli/tests/record.rs`, 6 tests, red first as `unknown method record` (`0 passed; 6 failed`), then `6 passed`; one of them types a secret during a recording and reads the steps file. MCP: `screenshot_with_record_starts_and_stops_a_video_and_returns_text_only`.
- Live on the API 37 emulator (win-pc, daemon on the mini, debug build), files in `~/pd-rec` on the mini. Four recordings, and two faults found by comparing the video's length with the wall clock:
  - Run 2: 25.3 s of video for 24.2 s of wall clock. The reader was about a second behind the stream when the recording started (a debug build decodes slowly, and the reader waits for the decoder's queue), so a second from before the start was in the file. Fix: `AndroidDevice` waits for a `video_barrier` before a start and before a stop, as a screenshot does.
  - Run 3: 5.5 s for 4.85 s. The encoder's time stamps ran ahead of the clock; the emulator's own clock did not (`/proc/uptime` moved 20.09 s in 20.09 s of host time). Frames repeated for an idle screen are stamped exactly 100 ms apart. Red test first (`the video is as long as the recording ran: 1725 ms against 1099`), then: a picture is placed by the gap between stamps and never later than the moment it was read.
  - Run 4, after both: `phonedriver record stop` printed `15.2 s, 1080x2424, 150 frames, 3.6 MB` and `steps: 3 with their times in …/run4.mp4.steps.jsonl`; the wall clock between the commands was 15.14 to 15.24 s. `ffprobe`: `codec_name=h264`, `width=1080`, `height=2424`, `duration=15.180356`, `nb_frames=150`. `ffmpeg -i run4.mp4 -f null -` exits 0 and a re-encode to 30 frames per second gives 455 frames in 15.17 s. Steps: `launched Settings` at 14 ms, `pressed back` at 6404, `scrolled down` at 11119; the tap between them found nothing and is not a step. I looked at a sheet of frames taken every 2 s: home screen, Settings at 2 and 4 s, home again from 6 s, the app drawer at 12 and 14 s, which is what those three actions do.
  - Run 1 showed what the feature is for: Settings sat on its splash screen for the whole 30 s on a cold emulator, which the replies had only reported as `still animating after 7317ms` and two failed actions.
- Whole run on the mini (`ulimit -n 8192`): `cargo clippy --locked --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean; `cargo test --locked --workspace --no-fail-fast`: 845 passed, 2 failed: `no_device_exits_4_with_the_message_then_the_hint` (devices are connected to the mini) and `a_frame_of_412_by_915_takes_under_120_ms_at_the_median` (an OCR speed limit, on a machine at load 4.7). In the run before it, six OCR tests failed with `Apple Vision could not read this screenshot`, the machine fault of the two entries above; they passed in this one.
- Not done: no physical phone (its hardware encoder may stamp frames honestly or not; the placement rule covers both, but nothing shows it). No iOS. A turned screen ends the recording instead of continuing. A killed daemon leaves a file without its index. `ffmpeg`'s null muxer warns about equal time stamps when it squeezes the first group (many pictures in 11 microseconds each) into its own coarse clock; the file's own clock is strictly increasing. No agent run shows an agent using `record` unasked or asked. ## 2026-11-15: the Mac reads a screenshot at the Fast level when the Accurate level refuses (D64)

- Why: the user asked for the Mac OCR failure to be fixed ("no passing run on the last commit").
- What the fault is. Reproduced outside phonedriver with a 30-line Swift loop. On the mini every program's `~/Library/Caches/<program>/com.apple.e5rt.e5bundlecache` is an empty folder (15 of them, Apple's `duetexpertd` and `visualintelligenced` among them); the MacBook writes 4 files (76 KB) there. With no cache each process compiles the model on its first call (13 s) and the Accurate level then works until the process sits idle for 8 s; after that every call fails in about 10 ms with `CRImageReaderError 1`. With a cache (MacBook, second run of the same program): first call 200 ms, 60 calls in a row and calls 2 s and 9 s apart all read. The mini has 2 to 3 GB free of 228; I did not prove the disk is the cause.
- Built: `recognize` on macOS falls back to `recognize_quick` (Vision's Fast level on the picture enlarged to about three pixels a point through Core Image). `objc2-core-image` and `objc2-core-foundation` are now named in `crates/cli/Cargo.toml`; both were already in `Cargo.lock` under `objc2-vision`, no package was added.
- Measured before choosing (Swift, MacBook, taps tab at one pixel a point, 30 strings): Fast as it is 20 exact, enlarged twice 26, three times 28, in 31 to 48 ms; Settings 17 of 17 and the iOS testbed 5 of 5 at every size. Nearest-neighbour enlarging read 22 at twice and 24 at four times, so the enlarging is Core Image's.
- Red: the six OCR tests that failed in three whole runs on the mini (entry above). The new test `the_quick_reader_reads_the_same_pictures_when_the_accurate_one_cannot` was written with the code and passed on its first run, so it never ran red.
- Proof on the mini, a throwaway probe (40 calls of `recognize`, 8 s pause after call 20; not in the repo), three runs, same result each: `A13648(30) A40(30) … A44(30) q61(30) q36(30) … q16(30)`. `A` is a read that has `Busy` (the Accurate level), `q` one that does not (the Fast level). 20 and 20, no `FAIL`. Before this change calls 21 to 40 were the error.
- On the mini: `cargo fmt --check` clean, `cargo clippy --locked -p phonedriver --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean, `cargo test --locked -p phonedriver --test ocr --test mcp`: 8 passed and 43 passed (`p50 63.4 ms` for the speed test).
- Not done: the whole suite was not run. The 13 s first read of every new process on the mini stays. Nothing tells the agent which level read the screen. The cause inside macOS is not found.

## 2026-11-15: a secret is masked wherever it comes back (D70), found by a property test

- Why: the user invoked `/create-formal-verification-skill`. I chose the promise in AGENTS.md that text passed with `--secret` never reaches logs, traces, errors or test output: a break prints someone's password into an agent transcript, and the example tests each checked one reply. Nothing verifies this Rust for every input, so the check is a property test of the real daemon against the fake phone (`crates/cli/tests/secret_property.rs`), not a model.
- The check, first run, 3,000 generated sessions (`survey`): 18 kinds of leak, four causes. F1: a secret typed into a field that shows its text was masked in that reply only; the next `snapshot`, `find` or action reply printed it (108 sessions through a snapshot reply), and `--trace-full` wrote it to disk (217). F2: a secret `type` as a `batch` step was not masked: `[Type { flagged: true, into: Note, in_batch: true }]` returned `~ @e1 textbox "Note" "Ty0mW4zR8nB3c" focused` (only a client other than the CLI and MCP can send that). F3: after a secret `type` whose tap moved the focus and whose text then failed, a plain `type` with no target trusted the old screen and pasted into the password field (1 session in 3,000). F4: the fake's call log printed a `shell --secret` command.
- Built (test first: the property, three unit tests in `daemon/secrets.rs`, two example tests in `tests/daemon.rs`, all red before): `daemon::secrets` keeps every secret the daemon is given, in memory, and `Daemon::call` masks them in every string of every reply and error before it is returned or traced. The mask threaded through `finish` and `report` and the one in `shell` are deleted (33 lines added to the daemon, 50 removed). `Session::unsure` makes a `type` with no target read the screen again after a failed action. `FakeDevice` logs a `ShellRequest`.
- Two things in my first oracle were wrong and are fixed in the test, not the product: it counted text aimed at the password field without `secret` as a secret (the daemon refuses that text, so it never had it), and the fake printed back texts it had never been sent. The check is now two properties: a secret never comes back, and the password field takes only secrets.
- Mac mini: `PROPTEST_CASES=4000 cargo test --locked -p phonedriver --test secret_property -- --include-ignored`: `0 kinds of leak`, `test result: ok. 4 passed; 0 failed`. `cargo test --locked -p phonedriver -p phonedriver-core --no-fail-fast`: lib 100, daemon 170, mcp 43, guard 38, guard_property 7, trace 8, shell 7, record 6, replay 5 and the rest passed, core 213; 1 failed, `no_device_exits_4_with_the_message_then_the_hint`, which fails there whenever a simulator is booted. Clippy for the workspace clean.
- Ten breaks of the product code each make the check fail (`python3 .claude/skills/verify-secrets/breaks.py <name>`, run on the mini): replies not masked, a secret forgotten after its call, a batch step or a shell command not read, the trace keeping typed text or a secret command, either `Debug` printing its text, the old focus trusted after a failure, plain text let into a password field.
- A false alarm on the way, mine: after the breaks ran on the mini I synced with `rsync -a`, which set the restored file's time back, so cargo kept the build with a break in it and the whole-suite run failed the property. The sync now compares content and leaves times alone.
- Not covered: pixels (screenshots, recordings), a secret under four characters after its own call, a secret the device prints back in a changed form, the CLI and MCP layers, a real device. The skill lists the rest.

## 2026-11-15: what three reviews of the day's work found, and what was fixed

- Why: the user asked for `/ponytail`, `/code-deslop` and `/code-review` and to clean up the codebase. Two read-only sub-agents read this branch's changes, one against AGENTS.md and the smell list, one against the decisions as the spec. This entry covers the fixes to code that was already committed; the fixes to the page read are in its own commit.
- `save` with nothing new emptied the file: it opened the file for writing before it asked for the steps. It now opens without truncating and writes only when there are steps; the reply ends `(<path> is as it was)`. The save test in `--test mcp` saves twice and reads the file after the second.
- `setting open` was written to the journal twice, once by `setting` and once by the action it runs through, so a saved run opened the screen twice. `--test setting`: the journal holds exactly 1 step after an `open`.
- F5, a secret on disk (D70 has the paragraph): the steps file of a recording is written inside the actor, before `Daemon::call` masks anything, so a tap on a label that showed a remembered secret wrote it to `<video>.steps.jsonl`. The actor now holds the daemon's list and masks the summary. The property test's phone has a label that repeats the note field, a step that taps it while recording, and a fixed session for it; break B11 (`python3 .claude/skills/verify-secrets/breaks.py B11-the-steps-file-of-a-recording-is-not-masked`) fails with `F5 … rec0.mp4.steps.jsonl holds the secret Ty1mW4zR8nB3c`.
- `record start` onto a path that had a steps file from an earlier recording kept appending to it. It now removes the old file first and refuses if it cannot.
- On a screen with no element list, an unverified action's own hint was dropped in favour of the empty-screen hint. Both are printed now, the action's first.
- `probe` in `crates/video/src/record.rs` multiplied a time stamp by 1000 unchecked; it is `checked_mul` now. `nal_units` is private.
- Comments: two doc comments in `crates/android/src/device.rs` had ended up above the wrong items and are back; a narration comment and a binding used once are gone from `actor.rs`. The test's enum `Into`, which hid `std::convert::Into`, is `Aim`.
- Docs that had gone stale: README (a fingerprint or Face ID check can be passed on an emulator or Simulator, not on a real phone; OCR on Linux needs Tesseract), `docs/architecture.md` (OCR per system; `pair | untether` in the verb list), `docs/reference.md` (a guarded setting answers `unsupported` without `--force`; a section on screens with no element list), the MCP `record` argument (Android and the iOS Simulator).
- Reported and left, because each is taste or a larger change than a review fix: one `SettingReply` type for four reply shapes, with guards in `setting()` that cannot be reached; two conversions of `Recorded`; an `Error::refused` helper for 30-odd literal constructions; three copies of the "one of find, key, values, open" check; the default `since` written in two places; `Taping::step` ignores a failed write (a full disk loses steps silently); a crash log that cannot be read after an action is ignored, and the 2 s `CRASH_MARGIN` has no observation beside it; D62's picture is not attached to `shell`, `setting`, `app` or `replay` replies; MCP marks `screenshot` read-only though its `record` argument writes a file on the host; `recordVideo --force` overwrites an existing file on a Simulator and the docs do not say so; `setting`'s unknown-key suggestions match by substring only; a `setting values` call that fails half way names what was set only when the failure is a refusal; D59's text predates the Simulator recording (D66 has it).
- Mac mini, this commit's tree by itself (the page read is not in it): `cargo fmt --all -- --check` clean, `cargo clippy --locked --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean, `cargo test --locked --workspace --no-fail-fast`: 876 passed, 1 failed, 130 ignored. The one failure is `no_device_exits_4_with_the_message_then_the_hint`, which fails there whenever a simulator is booted (`error: 3 devices are connected; pick one`).

- Shape: a device id that ends in `+<name>` is a lane (`lane_of` in `crates/core/src/device.rs`). The daemon gives it its own actor and lease and always connects it hidden. ## 2026-11-16: a call on a link that stalled ends in bounded time

- Seen in a lanes run: the ssh tunnel to win-pc's adb server stalled without closing, and `tap` waited 631 s (`round 1: 631.3 s | ... | error: device android:emulator-5554+b disconnected`), with `devices` queued behind it. `Control::request` waited for the reply with no limit.
- Now a request says how long it may itself run on the device (zero for most, the limit of a shell command, the length of a gesture), and a reply that has not come 30 s after that is `DeviceGone`, which makes the actor reconnect on the next call (`crates/android/src/control.rs`; the row in architecture section 8).
- Tests (`control::tests`, the crate's first with paused time, so `tokio`'s `test-util` is a dev-dependency there): a request never answered ends as a gone device after exactly its own 60 s plus 30; a reply with the request's id is the answer. I wrote them with the change; to see the first one fail I set the margin to 31 s on the mini only: `left: 91s right: 90s`.
- Mac mini: `phonedriver-android --lib` 64 passed; `--test daemon` 175 passed; `--test lanes` 7 passed; clippy clean.

## 2026-11-16: a screenshot is the screen after the action, also when the encoder is behind (D74)

- D73's open defect, followed to the end with a logging build (`docs/spikes/video-lag.md`). Three causes, one on top of the other.
- **The barrier did not wait.** Host and agent log side by side: `PDLAG android:emulator-5554+b id 3 reply {"id":32,"ok":true,"wait_ms":1050} ... took 33.889458ms ok true` against the agent's `A b id 32 ... B b id 32 asked 73806 waited 459`. The host asked for barrier 3; `Control::request` overwrote the request's `id` with its own, 32; the agent wrote 32; the host waits for `>= 3`, and 18 had already passed. So since 06bab61 (2026-11-08, releases 0.1.2 to 0.1.4) only a connection's first screenshot waited for its barrier. The number now travels as `barrier`, and `Control::request` has a debug assertion against a request that brings an `id`.
- **The encoder holds frames.** Per frame on two lanes after a tap: `f b lag 46`, `200`, `537`, `1198`, `1450`, then down again at about 30 frames a second. The agent wrote the barrier after the output on hand, so those frames followed it. It now holds the barrier while the last frame was more than 100 ms late, until a frame presented after the request is written or the encoder has been silent for 100 ms plus twice the lateness, and answers `wait_ms` so the host waits that much longer (`Video.java`, `caught_up` in `crates/android/src/device.rs`). My first rule, one decaying estimate of the encode time, let a barrier go in a 216 ms stall (`B b waited 569 by quiet sinceWrite 179 est 77`, next frame `lag 943`); replaced.
- **A software encoder is too slow at full size.** One lane, median time in the encoder: 598 ms at 1080x2424 and 30 frames a second, 43 ms at 822x1846 and 15. A hidden display on a software-only encoder opens at the second (`Hidden.java`).
- Tests first: `a_barrier_request_carries_its_number_apart_from_the_requests_own_id` failed with `left: Null right: 700`; `a_barrier_the_agent_says_is_behind_is_waited_for_that_much_longer` failed with `DeviceGone`. Mac mini: `phonedriver-android` 66 passed, `phonedriver-video` 13, `--test daemon` 175, `--test hidden_display` 6, `--test lanes` 7; clippy clean.
- Live, API 37 emulator, guest io `avg10` 0, a screenshot of lane `b` right after each of five taps while lane `a` taps too (`pd-duo.sh` on the mini, PNG size tells the page from the list): before `1 of 5` showed the page (`106795 117750 120352 118268` bytes are the list), after `5 of 5` (`66005 68794 71089 71655 71015`, three byte-identical to the shot two seconds later), in 0.61 to 1.11 s each. With the phone's own screen showing Clock as a third stream: `5 of 5` in 1.41 to 1.73 s, where the same run gave 1 of 10 before. The phone's own screen alone: `5 of 5`, 0.48 to 0.74 s after a tap and 0.15 to 0.21 s on a still screen. The testbed logged `b1` to `b5` in every run.
- I looked at four of the pictures side by side (two of lane `b` right after a tap, the still lane after `back`, one of the phone's own screen): the first two and the last are the Network & internet page, sharp at one pixel a point, the third is the Settings list. The rest I judged by size: the page and the list differ by 40 KB and the later shot of each round is the reference. Not done: the phone's own screen keeps the full-size stream on an emulator (recordings come from it), so a screenshot right after an action there waits about half a second; nothing was run on a physical phone, where the rule for an encoder that keeps up applies and is the old path.

## 2026-11-16: `compare`, what changed between two screenshots as text (D76)

- Asked for: "Screenshot diff that reports changed text", after the look at argent. Built for the agent's case, not for regression tests: on a screen with no element list, "did that do anything" cost a second picture.
- `phonedriver compare <before.png> [after.png] [--image out.png]` and MCP `screenshot` with `compare: true` (`crates/cli/src/compare.rs`, `commands.rs`, `mcp.rs`). Pixels decide whether and where; text is read only inside changed regions. The threshold (4 of 255 mean brightness in an 8 by 8 cell) is from thirteen real pairs: unchanged screens at most 2.6, real changes at least 9.3.
- Mac mini: `cargo test -p phonedriver --lib compare::` 7 passed; `--test mcp` 45 passed; `--test cli compare` 1 passed; clippy clean.
- Live, iPhone 17 Simulator, real MCP server: `screenshot {}` 115,912 bytes; `screenshot {"compare": true}` -> `same`, 32 bytes, 0.24 s; after `tap General` -> `changed: 1 region, 49.3% of the screen`, twelve lines, `and 14 more`, 563 bytes. Command line: `tap "Auto-Capitalisation"` then `compare kb-1.png` -> `changed: 1 region, 0.6% of the screen` / `region 304,592 64x40 (no text changed)`; tapped again -> `same` (the switch is back on).
- I looked at the two boxed pictures (`--image`): the General page with one box around the changed area, and the Keyboards page with one box exactly on the Auto-Capitalisation switch. The first live run drew three boxes, two inside the first; a failing test and a join of overlapping regions fixed that.
- Not good yet: the mini's recognition runs at the Fast level and misreads small marks (`5G .ll`); its first read in a process takes 14 to 17 s; a clock that moved is reported. Android proven on stored screenshots only (no emulator today). No agent run uses `compare` yet, so the saving is in bytes, not measured tokens.

## 2026-11-16: search and answer-engine research, a baseline before Show HN, three small edits

- Research: five lines of reading (how answer engines pick sources, new-domain indexing and entity, this niche's results and demand, technical and content playbooks, agent discovery and measurement) and one report. Local only, in `.context/research/` (not committed). What it found: Google documents that AI Overviews and AI Mode need only an indexed page that can show a snippet; no engine publishes an age or trust threshold; the best matched study of `llms.txt` found no effect on citations and log studies found crawlers almost never ask for it; FAQ rich results ended on 7 May 2026; a SoftwareApplication rich result needs a rating or review, which the site will not invent. The site already carries what has support (visible dates, on-page contents lists, dated comparison tables with a section where the rival wins, a Markdown link per page), so the open gaps are index coverage, the shared name, and measurement.
- Baseline, read on 16 November through the signed-in browser, the day before Show HN:

## 2026-11-16: an iPhone runner whose signature has run out is signed again by the next command (D84)

- Asked: a user complained that a free Apple ID's signing expires every 7 days; build the automatic re-sign first.
- What was there: `build_signed` used an existing device build whatever its profile said, so the README's "then `phonedriver ios setup` signs it again" was not true. `UserAction::ResignRunner` existed, was returned by nothing, and its hint said `doctor` re-signs, which it does not.
- Built: `profile_expiry`, `expired`, `reusable` and `stale_builds` in `crates/ios/src/runner.rs`; a signed build whose `ExpirationDate` has passed is built again; builds of other sources are removed for devices too. `doctor` reads the profile of this version's build without `security cms` and warns when it has run out. `ResignRunner` removed.
- Tests first, seen failing (the functions did not exist; doctor said "the profile's expiry cannot be read"): `a_date_in_a_profile_is_read_as_seconds_since_1970`, `a_signed_build_is_used_again_until_its_profile_runs_out`, `builds_of_other_sources_are_stale_for_the_simulator_and_for_devices`, `a_runner_whose_profile_has_run_out_says_the_next_command_signs_it_again`. On the mini: `cargo test -p phonedriver-ios --lib` 71 passed, `cargo test -p phonedriver-core` 215 passed, `cargo test -p phonedriver --lib doctor` 7 passed, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets` prints nothing.
- Proof on the MacBook with the iPhone 13 Pro Max attached (team <team id>, a paid team, profile to 2027-11-12). The new binary's first `ios setup` built the runner for the changed sources in 12.1 s and left two builds of 53 MB each; with both there, `doctor` read the old one's profile, which is why it now reads this version's. Then, with the date in the cached profile changed by hand to 2026-11-15:
  - `phonedriver doctor --device ios:00008110-001E04900239801E` -> `warn  ... runner  the signing profile ran out on 2026-11-15T20:35:22Z` / `fix: Nothing to do: the next phonedriver command on the device signs the runner again and installs it ...`
  - `phonedriver ios setup` -> `runner: building, installing and starting it` then `need ... The device is locked.`, 8.4 s; the profile file was written again and `doctor` says `ok ... signed, profile expires 2027-11-12T20:35:22Z`; `~/.phonedriver/runner` holds one build.
  - `phonedriver ios setup` again -> 2.9 s, nothing built.
- Not seen: a free Apple ID (whether Xcode issues the new seven-day profile in that build), and the install and start on the phone, which was locked for the whole session. The owner was asked to unlock it.
- Whole workspace on the mini afterwards: `cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast` fails one test, `no_device_exits_4_with_the_message_then_the_hint`, as on every run there (it needs a machine with no simulator booted; it passes in CI); every other test binary ends `ok`.

## 2026-11-16: simulator-use's benchmark steps run through phonedriver, and five things it showed (D85)

- Asked: "https://simulator-use.dev/benchmarks more competitor? lets mog this one too", benchmarks to be published after the release. So this was a private run to find what to fix first.
- Read their page and harness (`bench/run.mjs`, `bench/lib/measure.mjs`, the adapters, `examples/swiftui-demo`). Built the demo app on the Mac mini for the iPhone 17 Simulator and drove `phonedriver mcp` through the same steps with `bench/simulator-use/probe.py`.
- First run: tool list 1,914 tokens (theirs 2,443), read 82 tokens in 0.17 s (theirs 72 in 0.30 s), tap 41 (17), type 60 (14), sign-in 300 tokens in 4.06 s (194 in 4.28 s), and the batched sign-in refused: `step 2: secret text cannot go in a batch`.
- Cause of the long replies, by hand: `tap @e7` renumbered the tab bar (`- @e8 @e10 @e11`, `+ @e13 button "Login" selected`, `+ @e14 button "Items"`), `tap 201,350` did not. `--json snapshot` before and after: the two buttons' `id` went from `null` to `person` and `list.bullet`.

## 2026-11-16: SpringBoard's permission prompt is listed once

The location permission of Maps on that Simulator is left at "not allowed".

Input on a hidden display is not use of the phone's own screen, so it does not hold the screen-off timer: in hidden mode a phone with a PIN works for its own screen timeout after the owner puts it down. Not changed here; the release notes and the README say the phone has to be unlocked.

## 2026-11-16: an Android phone in use stays awake, on its own screen and in hidden mode (D86)

- Asked: "can u do like keepawake", after the HONOR X8 locked under an agent working on the hidden display.
- Found: the shell user holds `android.permission.DEVICE_POWER: granted=true` on the HONOR X8, and the vendored scrcpy `PowerManager` wrapper already has `userActivity(displayId)`. The host talks to the agent only when a call comes in, so the agent can tell use from idle by itself.
- Changed: `Control.usedAt` stamps each request; `Monitor` calls `userActivity(0)` every 10 s while the last request is under 10 minutes old, the phone is unlocked, its own screen is on and the lane has a connection. No host change, no new op.
- Test first: `a_phone_in_use_has_its_screen_timeout_reset_every_ten_seconds` in `crates/android/tests/agent_live.rs` (ignored; needs `PHONEDRIVER_TEST_DEVICE` and an unlocked phone; launches the bare agent, installs nothing). Built on the mini twice, with the old jar and the new one, and both run from the MacBook against the HONOR X8 by `scratchpad/awake/proof.sh` once the owner unlocked it:
  - old agent: `no user activity in 12 s with a host connected: 10812571 then 10812571`, `test result: FAILED`;
  - new agent: `test result: ok. 1 passed`.
- Then the CLI built with it, nobody touching the phone (`deviceLocked`, `mWakefulness` and `lastUserActivityTime` from `dumpsys trust` and `dumpsys power`):
  - hidden display, `launch Settings` and then no call: `21:47:26 deviceLocked=0 mWakefulness=Awake ... (2105 ms ago) hidden, 15 s` ... `21:48:12 deviceLocked=0 mWakefulness=Awake ... (7542 ms ago) hidden, 60 s`; `press back` ok; `release`.
  - own screen, the same: `21:48:33 ... (7482 ms ago) own screen, 15 s` ... `21:49:19 deviceLocked=0 mWakefulness=Awake ... (2385 ms ago) own screen, 60 s`; `press home` ok; `release`.
  - after release: `21:49:38 deviceLocked=0 ... (18413 ms ago)`, `21:49:53 deviceLocked=1 mWakefulness=Asleep ... (33795 ms ago)`.
  - a call on the locked phone: `press home` -> `error: The device is locked.`; host still connected: `21:50:18 deviceLocked=1 mWakefulness=Awake ... (8475 ms ago)`, `21:50:23 deviceLocked=1 mWakefulness=Asleep ... (12887 ms ago)`.
- Seen once, not explained: the first hidden `launch Settings` of that run answered `still animating after 6066ms, read as it is now`, where the run at 21:33 settled in 1384 ms.
- On the mini: `cargo clippy -p phonedriver-android --tests -- -D warnings` prints nothing; `rustfmt --check` on the test file passes. CI on `ca9a5b3` passed on all three systems before this change.
- Not run: an emulator (win-pc's emulator belongs to another session), Android before 12, a 15 s screen timeout.

## 2026-11-17: a GitHub Action that replays saved runs

- Asked: "make like phonedriver CI, just for people to use as a github actions".
- Added `action.yml` (composite, at the repository root, so `uses: hridaya423/phonedriver@<tag>`): installs a release (checked against its `.sha256`) or a given binary; starts an Android emulator on a Linux runner (KVM, `sdkmanager`, `avdmanager`, `-no-window`) or boots a Simulator on a macOS runner (`phonedriver boot`); installs `app`; replays each steps file; writes a row a run to the job summary; keeps each run's reply, and for a run that stopped its screenshot and logs, plus a video of every run with `record: true`, in an artifact; fails when a run stopped. Outputs `passed` and `failed`.
- `.github/workflows/replay-action.yml` runs it from this repository: Android with the testbed APK (`testbeds/android/runs/text.steps`), a run that must stop (`runs/missing/`, checked through the outputs), and iOS with `testbeds/ios/runs/settings.steps`, recorded on the iPhone 17 Simulator (`saved 5 steps`, then `replayed 5 steps in 9.6 s`). `actionlint` prints nothing.
- The replay step dry-run on the mini (the step's script pulled out of `action.yml`, GitHub's output and summary files faked, the Simulator, `record: true`), one run that works and one that taps a row that is not there: exit 0, outputs `passed=1` and `failed=1`, the summary table `replayed: replayed 5 steps in 11.2 s` and `stopped: step 2 of 2 (tap "No such row") failed: no element matches "No such row"`, and the folder held `ok.txt`, `ok.mp4`, `miss.txt`, `miss.mp4`, `miss.png` (looked at: Settings' main list, where the run stopped) and `miss.logs.txt`.
- `testbeds/android/runs/text.steps` was written by hand in the format `save` writes (win-pc's emulator was offline); the workflow is its first run.
- Docs: a "Replay in CI" page on the site, a section in the reference, the test-your-app page (save, replay, record, compare, permissions, crashes, turning the screen; its limit that said phonedriver does not replay is gone) and the Appium comparison.

## 2026-11-17: a replay checks each step's screen (D89)

- Asked overnight: features developers would love, with the GitHub Action. Planned as K1: a saved run that also checks what each screen shows.
- Added `replay <file> --screens <dir>`: the first replay keeps `01.png`, `02.png`, ... after each step; later ones compare each step's screen with its file through `compare` and stop at the first step whose text changed (pixels with no text do not stop it). `compare` now leaves out a status bar clock that moved (a time centred in the top tenth). The Action takes `screens`; a run with no subfolder keeps its screens in the artifact under `screens/`, and on Linux it installs Tesseract. A `wait` step is now described as `wait "Name"` in `save` and `replay` output, not as its JSON.
- Tests first: `a_status_bar_clock_that_moved_is_left_out_and_a_time_lower_down_is_not` (with the filter turned off on the mini's copy: `left: [Rect { x: 16.0, y: 16.0, w: 120.0, h: 40.0 }, Rect { x: 0.0, y: 400.0, ...`); `a_wait_step_is_described_as_the_command_that_waits` (failed with `left: "wait {\"condition\":{\"appears\":...`); `--test cli replay_with_screens_keeps_each_steps_screen_then_compares_with_it`.
- `cargo test -p phonedriver --no-fail-fast --lib --test cli --test mcp --test replay --test record` on the mini: 115, 21 of 22 (the known `no_device_exits_4...`, three devices attached there), 48, 6, 5. Clippy clean.

The Action's replay step run by hand: one pass kept two screens, a second pass with that folder matched both.
- Found while doing it, fixed in the same commit: the Action's replay step ran `shopt -s globstar`, which macOS's `/bin/bash` 3.2 refuses, and GitHub runs steps with `-e`, so the iOS job of `df3a662` failed there (`shopt: globstar: invalid shell option name`). The step now runs with `set +e` and tolerates a bash without `globstar`. The job summary said `replayed: replayed 2 steps`; it now says `replayed 2 steps`. The Android job of `df3a662` timed out after 600 s waiting for the emulator with nothing in the log; the emulator step now fails at once with the emulator's log if the emulator exits, and prints the log if it has not booted in 10 minutes.
- Seen once, not reproduced in four cold starts: a replay right after `daemon stop` failed at its first step with `device sim:E02F... disconnected`.
- Added `testbeds/android/runs/taps.steps` (restart, switch Dark theme, open List; no keyboard) and a second Action step in `replay-action.yml` that replays it against the screens the first step kept.

## 2026-11-17: the iOS runner no longer fills Xcode's DerivedData

- Found while freeing the Mac mini's disk (302 MB left): `~/Library/Developer/Xcode/DerivedData` held 84 `PhonedriverRunner-*` folders, 940 MB. Each is one runner session: `xcodebuild test-without-building` had no `-derivedDataPath`, so it made a new folder at every start, and when the runner was stopped it gathered `simctl_diagnostics` (the Simulator's system log archive, `system.logarchive` alone 95 MB) into its result bundle: 20 KB to 250 MB a session. A user's Mac gets the same from every session.
- Failing check first, on the mini with the debug build (`pd-dd-check.sh`: count the folders, `daemon stop`, `snapshot`, `daemon stop`, count): `before 83, after 84`.
- Changed (`crates/ios/src/runner.rs`): one `test_without_building` for the Simulator and the iPhone, with `-collect-test-diagnostics never` (in `xcodebuild -help` of Xcode 27.0) and `-derivedDataPath <home>/runner/<udid>.dd`, deleted before each start.
- After: `before 84, after 84`, and `runner/E02FE8A6-....dd` is 204 KB. The 67 folders older than 30 minutes were the mini's own leftovers from tests and were deleted (979 MB free after).
- Docs: architecture's Simulator lifecycle paragraph, CHANGELOG.
