## 2026-11-19: an outside review of 0.1.5, three pull requests merged and the rest fixed

- giulioc84 filed #13 to #18 and sent #19, #20 and #21. Each claim checked against `main` before anything else: true where checked, with correct line numbers. The three PRs merged after CI (including the iOS job of the replay Action, which ran #21's new step and replayed on the Simulator it booted). On the Mac mini, with the PRs merged: 970 passed, 0 failed; their two new tests fail with the fixes taken out (`expires_s: 29`, `a file outside the cleaned folder was deleted`).
- The rest on `fix/contributor-issues`, one commit per item, each test written first and seen failing for the reported reason (except the install record's daemon tests, which needed the changed `Device` API to compile): replay and a shell step's exit (#13), `save` that keeps the steps on a failed write, `record start` that cannot clear its steps file, the phone guard and saved `force`, the shell time limit and a stopped actor, the DevTools frame length, tap count and hold, Simulator `apps clear`, traces in the docs, MCP session state, the two leaking tests, the "Empty" field, the install record (#18), `doctor` naming the devices it starts the agent on, and `devices {use}`. Decisions: D16, D19, D21 and D60 amended, D97.
- #15 item 11, reproduced on an iOS 26.5 Simulator on the Mac mini (Xcode 27.0): `phonedriver shell "defaults read com.pd.check k"` answered `exit 111 · Process spawn via launchd failed. Invalid or missing Program/ProgramArguments`. Cause: iOS 26 runtimes have no `/bin/sh` (the runtime's `/bin` holds `df` and `launchctl`), and with Xcode 27 `simctl spawn` of any path inside the Simulator (`/usr/bin/defaults` too) fails the same way, while the same program by its path under the runtime root runs inside the Simulator (`log show` lists SpringBoard, `defaults` reads and writes the Simulator's own preferences). Fix: `shell` spawns the program by that path, through `<root>/bin/sh -c` where a runtime has a shell; without one, a command that needs a shell or names a program the Simulator lacks is refused with what it has. After: `defaults read com.pd.check k` printed `v`, a `defaults write` then read printed `fixed`, `launchctl print system/com.apple.SpringBoard` and `log show --predicate 'process == "SpringBoard"'` printed their output, `defaults read … | head -1` and `ls /` were refused as refusals, `simctl ui appearance` printed `light`. The throwaway Simulator was deleted.
- #16 item 4, a web page's fixed bottom bar over a tile: three Chrome trees recorded on the API 37 emulator from a page with a `position:fixed` nav bar (`chrome_fixed_bar*.json`: a tile fully under the bar, one with its centre showing, one with only a strip showing). Before: `tap` aimed at 863 pt, under the bar, and `scroll --until` said `already on screen, no scroll`. Live with the 0.1.5 build and the strip showing: `tap "Post 16"` answered `ok tapped @e27 "Post 16" · no change after 543ms`, a tap on the bar. After: the tap goes to the middle of the strip, and `scroll --until` keeps going until the tile clears the bar; the tests on the three trees pass and the Wikipedia golden is unchanged. The same live run with the fixed build did not finish: the emulator was under other builds' I/O load, then another UiAutomation client held it. Decision: D97 amended.
- #17: feature ideas, a reply posted.

## 2026-11-20: video on Samsung's display HAL through an OpenGL relay (#22)

- DrogaBox found and fixed it on a Galaxy A53 (Exynos 1280, One UI 8, Android 16): every frame of a virtual display rendering into the encoder failed in the HAL (`deliverWinConfigData failed: Invalid argument`), so `screenshot` timed out and `record` wrote 0 frames. Their patch, applied as sent except that the no-output fallback skips software encoders (on a loaded API 37 emulator a first frame can take seconds) and shorter comments: `GlRelay` between the display and the encoder, on for Samsung, `debug.phonedriver.gl` to force it, and a fallback after 1.5 s of no output from a hardware encoder. Their results on the A53: `doctor` video frame after 7 to 59 ms, `screenshot` 41 to 47 ms warm, `record` 79 frames in 2.6 s, hidden display works, no HAL errors.
- Here, API 37 emulator (no Samsung phone): `setprop debug.phonedriver.gl 1`, then `phonedriver doctor`: `ok video … a frame arrived after 61 ms` (43 ms on a second run); logcat showed the display's consumer as `SurfaceTexture-1-4666-0`. `screenshot`: 411x923, looked at, the home screen, upright, the same as with `0` (`a frame arrived after 101 ms`). `swipe up` with settle: `ok swiped up · new screen` both ways. Under heavy I/O load (avg10 91) the forced relay gave `no frame within 1 s`.

## 2026-11-20: a saved secret names an environment variable, and a saved run may hold `{{name}}` (D98)

- Why: a run with a login could not replay in CI (the secret step stopped the replay for a person), and a run that signs up could not replay twice. Both are in the CLI's replay, so the MCP `replay` tool and the Action get them with no wire change.
- New: `crates/cli/src/variables.rs` (`fill`, `run_id`, `SECRET_PREFIX`); `Kept` keeps a typed secret as the `type` action without `text` and `save` adds `secret_env` (`PHONEDRIVER_SECRET_1`, `_2`, ...); `replay` reads that variable from its own process environment and sends it as a secret; `--var name=value` (MCP `vars`) and `{{run}}`; a `vars` input in `action.yml`. `action.yml` needed no change for the secrets: composite steps inherit the calling step's `env`. Docs: `docs/reference.md`, `docs/protocol.md`, `docs/architecture.md`, README, `site/content/docs/replay-in-ci.md`, both `SKILL.md`, the MCP `replay` description. Decision D98 (the `PHONEDRIVER_SECRET_` prefix is required so an edited steps file cannot type another variable of the process).
- Tests first, seen failing before the code: `cargo test -p phonedriver --test cli --test replay --test mcp -- secret var replay` gave `FAILED. 4 passed; 5 failed` in cli.rs: `error: unexpected argument '--var' found` (3), `the type action is malformed: missing field \`text\`` (2, the secret steps hand-written with `secret_env`), and `saved 1 step ... 1. a secret was typed here; type it with \`type --secret\`` for the save test. The old-format test passed before and after, as it should. Added in `crates/cli/tests/cli.rs`: `a_saved_secret_names_a_variable_and_a_replay_types_what_the_environment_holds` (a second daemon replays it with the variable set; the secret text is in no reply, no trace and no file under the home, the trace has `"secret_len":12`), `a_replay_stops_at_a_secret_whose_variable_is_not_set_and_names_it` (also an empty variable), `a_secret_may_only_come_from_a_variable_that_begins_phonedriver_secret`, `an_old_secret_step_still_stops_the_replay_for_a_person`, `var_fills_double_brace_names_and_run_is_new_in_every_replay`, `a_variable_nobody_gave_is_refused_before_the_first_step_and_every_one_is_named`; `mcp.rs`: `replay_takes_vars_and_refuses_a_file_whose_variables_are_not_all_given`; `replay.rs`: `a_typed_secret_is_a_step_with_its_text_left_out` (was `..._is_a_step_for_a_person...`); three unit tests in `variables.rs`. The CLI tests run the built binary with `env`, since the workspace forbids `unsafe` and `set_var` is unsafe.
- Mac mini, `cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast`: 1020 passed, 0 failed, 137 ignored. `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`: clean. `cargo fmt --check`: clean.
- Live, API 37 tablet emulator (`emulator-5558`, debug binary built on the mini, own `PHONEDRIVER_HOME`). Boot took about 25 minutes with the host under load; "Process system isn't responding" and then "System UI isn't responding" until `adb root` and `pkill -f com.android.systemui`. Saved a run in Settings search: `tap "Search Settings"`, `type "battery" into "Search settings"`, then `printf 'hunter2-test' | phonedriver type --secret --into "Search settings"`. `phonedriver save live.steps` printed `3. type secret $PHONEDRIVER_SECRET_1 into "Search settings"` and `the secret text is not in the file: set PHONEDRIVER_SECRET_1 before replaying (rename a variable in the file if you like)`; the file's third line holds `"secret":true,"secret_env":"PHONEDRIVER_SECRET_1"` and no text. I edited step 2 to `"text":"{{term}}-{{run}}"`.
- `phonedriver replay live.steps` (no `--var`): `error: live.steps needs 1 variable nobody gave: term`, `hint: Add --var term=VALUE (MCP: vars). {{run}} is filled in by phonedriver; variables are not secrets, so they may show in replies.`, no step ran.
- `phonedriver replay live.steps --var term=battery` with the variable unset: `error: step 3 of 3 (type secret $PHONEDRIVER_SECRET_1 into "Search settings") failed: PHONEDRIVER_SECRET_1 is not set`, the two steps before it listed, the screen (`@e13 textbox "battery-71ad12be"`), `hint: Set PHONEDRIVER_SECRET_1 and replay again, or type the secret yourself with \`type --secret\`; it is the last step.`
- `PHONEDRIVER_SECRET_1=hunter2-test phonedriver replay live.steps --var term=battery`: `replayed 3 steps in 4.1 s (run 93c5e16e)`, the screen showed `@e13 textbox "•••"` and `No results for •••`. Run ids of the three replays: `e7c09fff`, `71ad12be`, `93c5e16e`. `grep -r hunter2-test` over the home and both steps files: no match; the traces have `"secret_len":12` three times.
- Limits seen: Settings did not return to its first page between replays here (stopping an app does not close the search it was in), so step 1 tapped the open search box, shown as `tapped @e13`; the typing worked the same. The first plain `type "battery"` while saving showed `batterybattery` (not verified, `still animating`) on the loaded emulator; that is the existing typing behaviour under load, not this change. ## 2026-11-20: a flaky guard test, and `apps notify` for push notifications (D100)

- Flaky test: `point_taps_in_a_row_read_the_screen_once_until_the_screen_is_reshaped` failed once on GitHub's Windows runner (`left: 2, right: 1`). The three `sleep(100ms)` calls stood in for "the daemon has taken the pushed frame in", but the daemon also reuses the guard's screen read for one second of wall clock (`GUARD_VIEW_REUSE`), and a slow machine can pass that second between the first tap and the small-frame tap. Reproduced on the mini by stretching the sleeps to 1.1 s: `left: 2 right: 1`. The test now runs with `start_paused = true`: time moves only when the daemon is idle, which is exactly when it has absorbed the frame. Under paused time a `WindowChanged` emitted in the same instant as the last read is not "after" it, so a 100 ms sleep goes before that emit. `a_window_change_event_counts_as_the_first_change` and `a_new_screen_after_an_action_leaves_the_settle_time_to_the_action_line` (a real 20 ms sleep inside a 500 ms window) are paused too, and `a_closed_mcp_connection_drops_its_sessions_state_...` polls for its journal instead of sleeping 50 ms. The other sleeps in `daemon.rs` are already inside `start_paused` tests.
- Proof: 50 runs of the one test, `test result: ok. 1 passed` 50 of 50; `guard` binary `38 passed`; `a_window_change_event`, `a_new_screen_after` and `a_closed_mcp_connection` 20 runs each, all ok.
- `apps notify`: `Device::push` (default `unsupported`), `AppParams.payload`, an `apps` op in the daemon, CLI `apps notify <app> '<json>'` and `--file`, MCP `apps` `action: "notify"` with `payload`. Tests first: `apps_notify_hands_the_payload_to_the_device_and_says_which_app_got_it` and `apps_notify_without_a_payload_says_what_it_needs` failed with `the app action is install, stop, clear, uninstall, grant, revoke or notify` (the action was not accepted yet), then passed. `a_push_payload_must_be_json_with_an_aps_object` was written with its function in one edit and was not seen failing.
- Live, own Simulator `pd016-c` (iOS 26.5) with the iOS testbed built by `testbed.py install`: `phonedriver launch dev.phonedriver.testbed`, `tap Dialogs`, `tap 'Request notifications'`, `tap Allow`, then `phonedriver apps notify Testbed '{"aps":{"alert":{"title":"Order shipped","body":"Your pad thai is on its way"}},"order":"7"}'` printed `ok sent a push notification to Testbed`; the screenshot (looked at) shows the banner "Order shipped / Your pad thai is on its way" over the Dialogs tab, and `putb.log` gained `{"ev":"notification","state":"shown"}`. `'{"alert":"x"}'` printed `error: the push payload needs an \`aps\` object ... nothing was sent` (exit 1); `'{oops'` printed `the push payload is not valid JSON (key must be a string at line 1 column 2)`; `--file /tmp/pd016.json` printed `ok sent a push notification to Testbed`; no payload printed the three ways to give one.
- Android was not run: no app on the emulators has an FCM receiver, so a refusal would prove nothing; the reasoning is in D100 and the reply says it.

I read them for anything personal before saving: they hold the emulator's own SSID `AndroidWifi`, its MAC addresses (`02:15:b2:00:00:0x`), `epc.tmobile.com` (the emulator's APN) and package names. The screenshot was looked at: the dialog reads "Connection request", "VpnProbe wants to set up a VPN connection ...", Cancel and OK. The VPN was a throwaway `VpnService` app built with aapt2, javac, d8 and apksigner (not in the repository): it opens `tun0` for 10.77.0.0/24.

## 2026-11-20: a device list the daemon enforces (#26, D101)

- `PHONEDRIVER_ALLOW_DEVICES`, `PHONEDRIVER_DENY_DEVICES` and the file `<home>/devices` (`allow <id>` / `deny <id>`, `#` comments). One wrapper around the daemon's device source (`Gated`, `crates/cli/src/daemon/policy.rs`) filters `list` and `list_for` and refuses `connect`, `connect_on` and `boot`, so every path resolves through it. A new `DeviceSource::hardware_serial` (Android: one read-only `getprop ro.serialno`, kept per connection) lets a serial entry name the same phone over USB and Wi-Fi; it is read only when a rule could name a serial and the id did not settle it. A new daemon method `hidden` says what is hidden; `phonedriver devices` ends with `N device(s) hidden by the device list`, the MCP reply does not; `doctor` does not show or probe a hidden device. Rules and limits are in D101 and `docs/reference.md` ("Devices an agent must not touch").
- Tests first. `crates/cli/tests/device_list.rs` (14 tests, a fake source that records every `connect` and every serial read). With `permits` stubbed to `true`: `10 failed; 4 passed` (`left: ["android:emulator-5554", "android:emulator-5558"] right: ["android:emulator-5554"]`, `expected an error: Object {"exit": 0, ...}` for `shell --force` on a hidden device, and so on). With the check in: `13 passed` in that file at this commit, and the 136 library tests pass, among them the file parser and the entry matching. They cover: an allow list hides the others; a deny hides one; deny wins over allow; the environment's allow list and the file's both bind; a hardware-serial entry hides one phone under two adb ids (`android:ZZ1` and `android:192.168.1.20:5555`) and admits both; a device the id settles is never asked for its serial; `shell --force` on a hidden device is `-32001` and the fake saw no call; no `connect` ever reaches a hidden device; a hidden emulator is not booted; the file is read again when it changes; a typo in the file fails every call with the line number; devices that are not ready are judged by id for `doctor`.
- Live, two emulators up (`emulator-5554` API 37 phone, `emulator-5558` API 37 tablet), `phonedriver` built on the mini, own `PHONEDRIVER_HOME`, both emulators' `phonedriver-agent-*.jar` and `phonedriver-owner` removed from `/data/local/tmp` before the run (`rm`, then `ls | grep -c phonedriver` gave 0 on the tablet):

```
$ PHONEDRIVER_DENY_DEVICES=android:emulator-5558 phonedriver devices
ID                     MODEL                OS                   TIER   LEASE
android:emulator-5554  sdk_gphone64_x86_64  Android 17 (API 37)  agent  -
1 device hidden by the device list
$ phonedriver snapshot --device android:emulator-5558
error: no device "android:emulator-5558"; connected: android:emulator-5554 (sdk_gphone64_x86_64)
hint: Pass `--device <id>` (or set PHONEDRIVER_DEVICE) to one of: android:emulator-5554.
exit=4
$ phonedriver --device android:emulator-5558 shell --force "ls /sdcard"
error: no device "android:emulator-5558"; connected: android:emulator-5554 (sdk_gphone64_x86_64)
exit=4
$ adb -s emulator-5558 shell ls /data/local/tmp
dalvik-cache
mobilecli.dex
mobilecli.so
```

  No `phonedriver-agent-*.jar` and no `phonedriver-owner` on the tablet after the daemon had listed it, been asked for it by id and been asked to `shell --force` on it. With the list in the file instead (`deny EMULATOR37X2X13X0`, the value `getprop ro.serialno` gives) both emulators were hidden (`no devices` and `2 devices hidden by the device list`): the two emulators report the same `ro.serialno`, so a serial entry cannot tell emulators apart and D101 says to name them by id. A typo (`deney x`) in the file made `devices` fail with `line 1 is `deney x`, not `allow <id>` or `deny <id>`` and exit 1; removing the file brought both back on the same daemon.
- Found while testing live: my first version reused the one-per-phone helper, which takes any adb name without a colon as its own serial, so an emulator's `ro.serialno` was never read and the serial entry did nothing. The list now always reads the property (separate cache in `AdbSource`).
- Not covered, and said in D101: `pair`, `untether`, `ios setup`, `ios wifi` and `ios register` (run by a person on a device they name, outside the daemon); a running daemon keeps the environment it started with.

## 2026-11-20: `doctor` probes only devices nobody else owns (#27, D101)

- Without `--device`, `phonedriver doctor` (and `init`, which runs it) checks the host and lists every device, and starts the agent or runner only on an emulator or Simulator that no live session holds. A physical phone or tablet (Android or iOS) and any device a live session holds get one line, `not probed: <why>`, with `fix: Run `phonedriver doctor --device <id>` to probe it.` `--all-devices` probes physical devices too, still skipping held ones; `--device <id>` probes that device only, held or not. The stderr line `starting the agent on ...` now names only the devices that are probed. "Held" is the new `lease_live` on each `devices` entry (an MCP session's lease, or a command line's within `TERMINAL_HOLD` of its last call), so a lease that has already yielded does not block a probe. If the daemon cannot be asked, nothing is probed without `--device` and a `daemon` line says why. Decision in D101, text in `docs/reference.md` (doctor), `docs/protocol.md`, `site/content/docs` (install, troubleshooting, android-emulator, android-phone).
- Tests first (they did not compile before `Scope` and `Verdict` existed; the five new `doctor::tests` then passed): a physical phone, an address over Wi-Fi and an `ios:` device are listed and not probed (and the line carries the `--device` command); an emulator and a Simulator are probed; a held device is left alone, also with `--all-devices`, while `--device` still probes it; `--all-devices` probes the physical phone; `--device` probes exactly the named device; a hidden device is not shown; without the daemon nothing is probed. `crates/cli/tests/device_list.rs` gained `a_live_lease_is_marked_so_doctor_can_leave_the_device_alone` (a command-line session's lease from a snapshot shows `lease_live: true`).
- Workspace on the Mac mini (`cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast`): 1,076 passed, 0 failed, 137 ignored (the live and device tests). `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`: clean. `cargo fmt --check`: clean.
- Live, `emulator-5554` and `emulator-5558` up (the emulators were starved, `/proc/pressure/io` avg10 between 50 and 90 for the whole session while another build ran on win-pc, so several probes timed out in UiAutomation and are not shown; the lines below are the runs that completed), `PHONEDRIVER_DENY_DEVICES=android:emulator-5560` because a third emulator that is not mine was attached:

```
$ phonedriver doctor        (both emulators, nobody holds them)
starting the agent on android:emulator-5554, android:emulator-5558; `--device <id>` checks only one
ok    android:emulator-5554               connected
ok    agent android:emulator-5554         answers hello: sdk_gphone64_x86_64 on Android API 37, 26.0 s with deploy
ok    uiautomation android:emulator-5554  connected, the screen tree reads
ok    android:emulator-5558               connected
ok    agent android:emulator-5558         answers hello: sdk_gphone64_x86_64 on Android API 37, 1.0 s with deploy
ok    device list                         1 device hidden by the device list and not probed
$ phonedriver --session other snapshot --device android:emulator-5558 >/dev/null; phonedriver doctor
starting the agent on android:emulator-5554; `--device <id>` checks only one
ok    android:emulator-5558               connected
warn  agent android:emulator-5558         not probed: session other holds it, and probing would take its agent
      fix: Run `phonedriver doctor --device android:emulator-5558` to probe it.
$ (same, then) phonedriver doctor --all-devices          -> the same warn line for 5558; starting the agent on android:emulator-5554 only
$ (same, then) phonedriver doctor --device android:emulator-5558
ok    agent android:emulator-5558         answers hello: sdk_gphone64_x86_64 on Android API 37, 1.0 s with deploy
ok    uiautomation android:emulator-5558  connected, the screen tree reads
```

- A physical phone was not touched, so `not probed: a physical device` is shown by the fake-device tests only.
- A mistake to own: my first `doctor` with no list probed a third emulator on the shared adb server (`emulator-5560`, not one of mine), which is exactly what the new default allows for emulators nobody holds. It answered `Android's accessibility service is not running on emulator-5560` (it was still booting) and I touched it no further; my agent jar may have been pushed to it. I then denied it by id for the rest of the session.

## 2026-11-20: an app scope the daemon enforces (D102, #28)

`PHONEDRIVER_APP_SCOPE=com.example.app,...` (daemon environment, never a tool argument) limits the daemon to those apps. `ScopedDevice` (`crates/cli/src/daemon/scope.rs`) wraps the connected device; `core::scope::AppScope::redact` takes other apps' windows, the bars and unlisted nodes out of every tree. Design, rejected options and the iOS rule are in D102; every known gap is in `docs/reference.md`, App scope.

- Tests first: `crates/cli/tests/app_scope.rs` (21) and `core::scope` (8). With the wiring switched off the 21 gave 15 failures (the hello check and three in-scope cases passed); with it on, 21 pass. Two of my own mistakes showed up and were fixed: a `type` in another app said "no field has focus" before the scope's reason (the actor now checks first), and the empty-screen hint told the agent to screenshot or tap a screen it could not.
- Mini, `cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast`: 1082 passed, 0 failed, 137 ignored; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean; `cargo fmt --check` clean.
- Live, Pixel_API_31 emulator (emulator-5560), scope `com.android.settings`, daemon with `PHONEDRIVER_TRACE_FULL=1`: `launch Settings` and `snapshot` show Settings; `launch Chrome` -> `error: no app named "Chrome" is in the app scope (com.android.settings)...`; `shell` refused naming `daemon stop`; `press home` -> `app com.google.android.apps.nexuslauncher (outside the app scope)`, then `tap`, `type`, `screenshot` refused with the package named; `launch Settings` from there works. `cmd notification post -t Hi tag 'secret-text'` on API 31 is importance 3, so it makes no heads-up; with `cmd statusbar expand-notifications` the shade (`com.android.systemui`) is on screen: `snapshot` -> `app com.android.systemui (outside the app scope)`, `screenshot`, `tap`, `type` refused, `press back` returns to Settings, `screenshot` then works (image looked at: Settings, status bar icons only). `grep -rl secret-text` over the PHONEDRIVER_HOME (traces included) found nothing. Control without a scope: `find secret-text` -> `@e14 button "Shell" "Hi · secret-text"`. A share chooser (`ResolverActivity`, package `android`) was withheld as `app android (outside the app scope)`.
- Not done: no heads-up notification was produced (needs a high-importance channel), no live iOS run (fake-device tests only), the share sheet was tried only with the API 31 chooser. The coordinator's note about another agent's `doctor` probing 5560 during boot: my one early "agent did not print ready" error was in that window and was not counted; I removed `/data/local/tmp/phonedriver-owner` and re-ran the whole live script after it.

- Why: off a Mac a device needed the cable (D96). Spike W1 (`docs/spikes/ios.md`) found that Xcode's network pairing, made through `com.apple.dt.remotepairingdeviced.lockdown` with the code `000000`, needs no approval on the device and reaches testmanagerd and the lockdown shims over Wi-Fi.

- New: `remote` (record in `<home>/ios-wifi-pairing/<udid>.plist`, `pair`, `tunnel`, `announced_by`), `Link::Network { pairing }`, the `_remotepairing._tcp` browse in `lan`, `transport::Services` (lockdown or the tunnel) under setup's status, the Developer Mode switch, the mount, `find_installed`, `start_installed` and `apps`, `Host::Tunnel`, `UserAction::PairForWifi`, and `ios wifi on` pairing on the cable off a Mac (`ios wifi off` deletes the record). `transport::rsd_client` moved out of `coredevice`.

Seconds later the phone announced `_remotepairing._tcp` (port 49152, a new identifier `E3A314C3-…` against `9EDA089A-…` in the spike) and `_apple-mobdev2._tcp`, and `devices` printed `ios:<udid>  <name>  26.5  runner`.
  - `phonedriver doctor`: `paired … tier runner, over Wi-Fi`, `developer mode  on`, `developer disk image  mounted`, `runner  installed as dev.phonedriver.runner.xctrunner.<team>`.

## 2026-11-20: doctor says a locked phone plainly, and a stuck device no longer stalls `devices`

What went wrong, on the user's iPhone17,5 (iOS 26.5) on Wi-Fi from Windows 11:
- `phonedriver doctor` with the phone locked printed `fail … developer services  Transport { detail: "… does not announce its remote pairing service; unlock it …" }` with `fix: Run \`phonedriver ios setup\`.`, and the runner line's text ended `Run \`phonedriver doctor\` to check the connection.`
- An older daemon from `~/.local/bin` (built 01:12, before the Wi-Fi work), started by another agent's MCP server, answered `snapshot` with `xcrun devicectl device info details: program not found`. Replaced that copy with the repo build (old one kept as `phonedriver-2026-11-20-0112.exe.bak`).
- At 09:32:12 the phone dropped off (`device … disconnected`). A later call's reconnect never returned, and at 09:34 `phonedriver devices` got no reply for over 3 minutes: it asked the device's actor for the lease (`Call::Leased`) and queued behind that call. `daemon.log` had no `starting the iOS runner` line after the drop, so the reconnect stopped before the runner start, in `mount_cached` → `setup::status`; the daemon held a second TCP connection to the phone (`netstat`).

Changes:
- `doctor.rs`: `ios_failed` says an iPhone check's error in words (`{e}`, not `{e:?}`) with the error's own fix unless that fix is to run doctor, else `REACH_FIX` (unlock, same network, or the cable). Used for `reachable`, `developer services` and `runner`; `find_runner` returns the typed error. `ios setup` prints its errors the same way.
- `daemon/actor.rs`, `daemon/mod.rs`: each actor publishes its lease and hidden display (`Held`, a `watch` channel) when a call takes the lease and after every call; `devices` reads that. `Call::Leased` is gone.
- `ios/setup.rs`: `status` off the `devicectl` path gives up after 15 s (`STATUS_WITHIN`) with `connection error: ios:<udid> did not answer within 15 s; unlock it …`.
- `docs/architecture.md`: the lease paragraph says how `devices` reads leases; the iOS failure table has a row for a tunnel that stalls without closing.

Tests (each failed first):
- `doctor::tests::a_locked_device_on_wifi_is_told_in_words_with_a_fix_that_names_neither_setup_nor_doctor`, `a_failure_that_needs_the_person_keeps_its_own_message_and_fix`: first run `cannot find function \`ios_failed\``.
- `daemon.rs::devices_answers_while_a_call_waits_on_a_device_that_never_connects` (the fake source's `connect` never returns): first run `devices waited for the call that never connects: Elapsed(())`.
- `setup::tests::a_device_that_stops_answering_fails_the_read_instead_of_waiting_for_good`: first run `cannot find function \`answered_within\``.

Commands (`PHONEDRIVER_AGENT_JAR` pointed at an empty placeholder file, as before):
- `cargo fmt --all --check`: clean. `cargo clippy --release -p phonedriver-ios -p phonedriver-core --all-targets -- -D warnings` and `cargo clippy --release -p phonedriver --no-default-features -- -D warnings`: clean.
- `cargo test --release -p phonedriver-ios -p phonedriver-core --lib`: `ok. 227 passed`, `ok. 91 passed`. `cargo test --release -p phonedriver --no-default-features --lib --test daemon --test mcp --test lanes --test device_choice --test hidden_display --test sources --test progress`: `ok. 107`, `183`, `7`, `7`, `7`, `48`, `3`, `8 passed`.
- `cargo build --release -p phonedriver --no-default-features`: `Finished`.

Live, the same phone, release build, read-only (no taps):
- Unlocked: first `snapshot` 12.8 s (connect 10.5 s), `devices` 22 ms.
- The user locked it at 10:29. A poll every 20 s for 6 minutes (daemon trace): the runner kept answering the lock screen (153 to 720 ms). The phone dropped off twice (10:29:43 after 6.9 s, 10:35:08 after 3.7 s, both `device … disconnected`); the next call reconnected while locked (10.2 s, and 18.9 s at 10:35:50). All 19 `devices` calls answered in 3 to 102 ms.

## 2026-11-20: opening a Wi-Fi tunnel gives up after 15 s

- A code review of the change found that `remote::tunnel` had no time limit outside `setup::status`, so `reveal_developer_mode`, `mount_files` and `start_installed` could still hang on a tunnel that stalls. That is the failure seen above, so it is fixed.
- Change: `answered_within` and its 15 s (`ANSWER_WITHIN`, was `setup::STATUS_WITHIN`) moved to `transport`. `remote::open` opens the tunnel within it, and `Services::open` uses it, so every Wi-Fi path off a Mac opens its tunnel under the limit. `remote::tunnel` is private.
- Test first: `remote::tests::a_device_that_takes_the_connection_and_never_answers_fails_the_tunnel_instead_of_waiting_for_good` opens a tunnel to a local socket that accepts and never answers. First run: `cannot find function \`open_within\``. Then it passed in 0.2 s.
- `cargo fmt --all --check`: clean. `cargo clippy --release -p phonedriver-ios -p phonedriver-core --all-targets -- -D warnings` and `-p phonedriver --no-default-features -- -D warnings`: clean. `cargo test --release -p phonedriver-ios -p phonedriver-core --lib`: `ok. 230 passed`, `ok. 98 passed`. `cargo test --release -p phonedriver --no-default-features --lib --test daemon --test device_choice --test hidden_display --test lanes --test mcp --test progress --test shell --test sources`: 117, 199, 7, 7, 7, 53, 3, 11 and 8 passed.
- Still without a limit: reads through a tunnel after it opened, other than Developer Mode and the mounted images (the runner's lookup in the installation proxy, the mount's upload). Not run on the phone.

## 2026-11-20: a debug `doctor` overflowed its stack on Windows

- After the 15 s limit above, CI's `test (windows-latest)` failed five `doctor` tests in `tests/cli.rs` (`doctor_probes_only_the_device_it_is_given` and four more) with empty stdout; the commit before it had passed. Locally the release build passed them; the debug build, which CI uses, printed `thread 'main' has overflowed its stack` for `phonedriver doctor`.
- Cause, measured with `size_of_val` in a debug test build: the tunnel future (`remote::tunnel`) is 84,224 bytes, and with the 15 s limit around it (`remote::open`) 168,928. Held inline, it made `Services::open` 169,552 and `setup::status` 339,808 bytes, and `doctor` holds several such futures on Windows' 1 MB main thread stack. Boxed in `Services::open`: 1,088 and 4,096.
- Change: `Services::open` awaits `Box::pin(remote::open(..))`, with a comment naming the stack limit.
- Before: `cargo test -p phonedriver --test cli doctor` (debug, Windows 11): `5 failed`. Boxing `doctor`'s `physical` instead did not help (`5 failed`). After: `ok. 5 passed`, then the whole file `ok. 22 passed`; on upstream `main` with this work, `cargo test -p phonedriver --test cli` (debug, Windows 11): `ok. 29 passed`. `cargo fmt --all --check`, both clippy commands with `-D warnings`: clean. `cargo test --release -p phonedriver-ios -p phonedriver-core --lib`: 227 and 92 passed.
- Not checked here: the rest of the CLI's tests in debug; the build stopped when this computer's drive filled up. CI runs them.
- Open: with the iPhone attached on Wi-Fi, a debug `phonedriver doctor` and `phonedriver ios setup` still overflowed the stack (both run once by mistake on the user's phone; each stopped with the overflow). The release build drives the phone. Not chased: it needs the phone.

## 2026-11-20: #29 taken into 0.1.6

- Rebased on the device list and app scope (#30). Its decision is D103 (D101 was taken). The actor's `Held`, which `devices` reads without waiting for the actor, now also carries whether the lease is a command line's, so `devices` still reports `lease_live`, which `doctor` uses to skip a device another session holds; the `Leased` call is gone.
- idevice comes from `hridaya423/idevice` (branch `phonedriver-run-over-rsd`), a copy of Coreho's commit `3bd5d45` on v0.1.68 under the project's own account, so a build does not depend on a contributor's repository.
- Windows gives a program's first thread 1 MB of stack; #30's debug build overflowed it before reading its arguments (28 of 30 `--test cli` failures on CI), and #29 had seen `doctor` overflow it with a phone on Wi-Fi. `phonedriver` now runs on a thread with a 16 MB stack.

## 2026-11-21: the guard checks every touch and enter (D104; GHSA-25p2-p3fx-5hmm, GHSA-rm5p-9fp9-hgrx)

An outside review (giulioc84, private advisories) showed a held payment going through with no prompt and no `autonomous`. All checked against `main` first and confirmed: the guard was called only from `tap` and `type --submit` (`guard_tap`, `guard_submit` in `actor.rs`).

What changed, per reported path:
- `type x --into "Pay €49.99"`, `clear "Pay €49.99"` (25p2 cases 1, 2): the focus tap goes through `guard_tap`.
- `type --into IBAN --submit` with a Pay button disabled until the field is filled (25p2 case 3): `submit_button` counts a disabled button; with no screen yet in the session it reads one.
- `press enter` after typing (25p2 case 4): `guard::entered` checks the focused button or the focused field's form.
- Zero-length `swipe` from "Place order", `drag` onto itself, one-finger `gesture`, `pinch` (rm5p, 25p2 R1): `guard::touched`, a finger that goes down and lifts on a guarded element is a click there. Slide-to-pay controls are held wherever the finger lifts; `drag` also counts where it drops.
- Unlabeled wordings (25p2 R2): `Pay with ...`, `Pay 500`, `kr`, `zł`, `AED` and more currencies; slider wordings in the eleven languages.
- A price inside a "Place order" row (25p2 R3): `guard::receiver` walks up to the element that takes the touch.
- Approval not bound to what was shown (25p2 R4): `approved` (label and screen lines) rides on `act`/`batch` after the human answers; a mismatch asks again.
- MCP `swipe`, `scroll`, `press` and clearing through `type` ask the human like `tap`; the CLI verbs take `--autonomous`.

Smoothness: an ordinary swipe, scroll or pinch reads the screen no more often than before (it uses the resolved element, the screen in hand, or the session's sure last screen). Test `an_ordinary_swipe_or_scroll_reads_the_screen_no_more_than_with_the_guard_off`.

Tests (on win-pc's WSL, `cargo test --locked -p phonedriver --test guard`):
- First run, new tests against the old `crates/cli/src` (stashed): `test result: FAILED. 38 passed; 9 failed`; each of the nine panicked at `expected a refusal, got Ok(...)` (`typing_into_a_guarded_button_is_held_before_its_focus_tap`, `clearing_...`, `submitting_a_form_whose_pay_button_waits_for_the_text_...`, `press_enter_after_typing_into_the_form_is_held`, `a_first_call_with_no_screen_read_yet_is_still_held`, `swipes_drags_pinches_and_gestures_that_click_a_guarded_button_are_held`, `dragging_onto_an_opted_in_delete_button_is_held`, `a_slide_to_pay_track_is_held_whatever_verb_moves_it`, `an_approval_covers_only_the_button_and_details_the_human_was_shown`).
- One more failure found on the way: a swipe past the end of a slide track lifted outside it and was not held; sliders now count wherever the finger lifts.
- After: `guard` 54 passed (with six MCP cases on both protocols: a held swipe, press and clear are asked and run in the same call, a declined swipe sends no touch, a client that cannot ask gets the question), `guard_property` 7, `mcp` 53, `daemon` 200, `cli` 30, lib 148 (guard unit tests: `a_finger_that_goes_down_and_lifts_on_a_guarded_button_is_a_click_however_it_moved`, `a_stroke_along_a_slide_to_pay_track_is_guarded`, `a_price_inside_a_place_order_row_commits_the_row`, `enter_sends_the_focused_fields_form_even_while_its_pay_button_is_disabled`, `enter_on_a_focused_pay_button_clicks_it`, and the new classifier rows).
- `cargo test --locked --workspace --no-fail-fast`: 60 test binaries `ok`, none failed. `cargo clippy --locked --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`: clean. `cargo fmt --all --check`: clean.

Not run on a device: whether a real slider or a real form behaves as the fakes do. The reporter's emulator runs showed the paths; these tests show the guard now holds them.

## 2026-11-21: 0.1.7 security, `shell` reaching the computer (D108)

- **GHSA-mfp4-j74c-44xh item 1** (Android emulator console). Verified on `origin/main`: `AndroidDevice::shell` sent any `emu …` text to `console::command_on`, and `run_on` wrote it with `\r\n` after it, so an embedded line break sent a second command. Change: `console::check` refuses any control character, always, and without `PHONEDRIVER_HOST_SHELL=1` sends only `geo`, `sms`, `power`, `network speed|delay|status`, `sensor`, `rotate`, `finger` and `avd name|status`; `command_on` checks the line again. `press fingerprint` goes through `touch_sensor_on`, not `check`, and is unchanged. The refusal is `refused` and names the offer and the switch, never the command.
- **GHSA-jg7g-xh43-x972 Part B** (Simulator). Verified: a command that is not `simctl` became `simctl spawn <udid> …`, and the allow-list held `spawn`, `io`, `addmedia`, `install`, `keychain` and `push`. Change: `simulator::shell_args` takes the switch; without it, it refuses a command that is not `simctl`, `simctl spawn`, `install`, `addmedia`, `push`, `io screenshot|recordVideo`, `keychain add-cert|add-root-cert`, `launch --stdout|--stderr` and `openurl file:`, each with why and the verb to use. phonedriver's own `log stream` (`wait log:`) builds its command with the new `spawn_args`. Every `shell` (CLI, MCP, replayed step) reaches the device's `shell` through the daemon's one `shell` method; `batch` takes no `shell` step.
- **Docs.** D108 (new); D6, D21 and D92 amended in place; `docs/reference.md` shell section and variable table; `docs/architecture.md` shell note; the CLI help; `SECURITY.md` (what the switch allows is not a vulnerability); site pages `shell.md`, `ios-simulator.md`, `android-emulator.md`.
- Test first, against stubs that accepted everything (WSL `pd-sec-host`): `cargo test -p phonedriver-android --lib console`: `FAILED. 7 passed; 3 failed`; `an_embedded_line_break_is_refused_and_nothing_reaches_the_console` got ``unwrap_err()` on an `Ok` value: ""`` (the report's finding), the two allow-list tests got `Ok(())`. `cargo test -p phonedriver-ios --lib simulator`: `FAILED. 16 passed; 2 failed` (`a_program_in_the_simulator_runs_on_the_mac_so_it_waits_for_the_persons_switch`, `simctl_forms_that_run_a_mac_program_or_name_a_mac_file_wait_for_the_persons_switch`). After: `ok. 10 passed` and `ok. 18 passed`. `mcp::a_shell_tool_call_and_a_replayed_shell_step_hand_the_device_the_same_command` checks the path, not the rule, and passed before and after.
- `cargo fmt --all --check`: clean. `cargo clippy --locked --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`: `Finished`. `cargo test --locked --workspace --lib`: `ok` for every crate (143, 87, 238, 102, 11). `cargo test --locked -p phonedriver --test mcp --test shell --test replay --test fingerprint --test cli`: `ok. 54`, `11`, `6`, `2`, `30 passed`. `cargo test -p phonedriver-core --lib` alone does not compile on `origin/main` either (`testing.rs` needs tokio's `time` feature, which the workspace build turns on).
- Not run: no emulator and no Simulator (`xcrun` does not run on Linux). Not covered: the `install` verb installs an `.app` from any path, which on a Simulator runs as the Mac's user; `open` with a `file:` URL goes through the iOS runner.

## 2026-11-21: 0.1.7 security, secrets and local files (D105; GHSA-522g-rv3m-hm5j, GHSA-g453-82w6-qqx5, #32)

Branch `sec/secrets`. Every item below was checked against the code first; each claim held. Tests run on win-pc's WSL Ubuntu (Linux), fake devices only, no phone.

What changed, by report item:
- 522g 1 (a secret given while a call waits): `Daemon::call` remembers what a call carries on arrival and reads the list again when the reply is ready; the trace uses that list. Test `netview.rs::a_secret_given_while_a_log_wait_is_pending_is_masked_in_that_waits_reply_and_trace`.
- 522g 2 (log line cut before masking): the actor masks the line, then cuts it at 400. The other cuts of device text before the daemon's mask (snapshot values at 200, `shell` lines at 300, `page` at 8,000, Android setting values at 120) all end in `…`; the mask now also hides the start of a secret, three characters or more, just before a `…`. Tests `netview.rs::a_log_line_is_masked_before_it_is_cut_so_no_part_of_a_secret_comes_back`, `daemon.rs::a_long_secret_that_the_snapshot_cuts_short_shows_no_part_of_itself`, `secrets::tests::a_secret_cut_short_before_an_ellipsis_is_masked_up_to_the_cut`.
- 522g 3 and g453 4b (comparison text): new daemon method `mask`; a `replay --screens` failure, CLI `compare <before>` and MCP `screenshot {compare: true}` go through it, and a replay also masks its failure with the secrets it read from the environment. Test `cli.rs::replay_screens_masks_the_text_it_read_from_a_screen_that_shows_the_secret` (Linux: a fake `tesseract` on `PATH` reads the secret from the second picture). A two-file `compare` is not masked (no daemon involved; D105 Limits).
- 522g 4 (MCP replay types any secret anywhere): a replay sends `secret_field_only` with a `secret_env` step; the daemon types it only into a password or one-time code field and refuses before tapping or typing otherwise. `PHONEDRIVER_REPLAY_SECRET_ANY_FIELD=1` where the replay runs lifts it. The rule is the same for the CLI and MCP: an agent with a shell runs the CLI too, and the documented login flow types into a password field. Tests `daemon.rs::a_replayed_secret_is_typed_only_into_a_password_or_one_time_code_field`, `cli.rs::a_replayed_secret_goes_only_into_a_password_or_code_field_unless_the_environment_allows_any`. The existing replay test now replays into a password field.
- 522g 5 (trace parameters): the trace masks remembered secrets in every method's sanitized parameters. Test `trace.rs::a_remembered_secret_is_masked_in_the_parameters_of_every_method_the_trace_keeps` (`apps notify` payload and a plain `shell`).
- 522g 6 and g453 4a (daemon environment): `client::daemon_env` drops `PHONEDRIVER_SECRET` and every `PHONEDRIVER_SECRET_*` (any case); Unix spawns with `env_clear().envs(..)`, Windows passes an explicit sorted UTF-16 block with `CREATE_UNICODE_ENVIRONMENT`. Tests `client::tests::the_daemon_is_given_no_phonedriver_secret_variable`, `a_spawned_daemon_sees_only_the_environment_it_is_given`, `cli.rs::the_daemon_a_cli_starts_inherits_no_phonedriver_secret_variable` (reads `/proc/<pid>/environ`). The Windows code was not compiled here: WSL has no `x86_64-pc-windows-gnu` target.
- 522g 7 (predictable `.tmp`): `save` stages through `tempfile` (`create_new`, random name) beside the file and renames it over. `tempfile` moved from dev-dependencies to dependencies (no lock change). Test `cli.rs::save_stages_its_file_under_a_fresh_name_and_follows_no_link_beside_it`.
- g453 2 (reformatted secret): the mask also matches a secret of six or more characters with spaces, `-` or parentheses between its characters, and after a secret `type` the actor remembers the field's shown value when it holds the typed characters in order in any case. Tests `daemon.rs::a_secret_the_field_reformats_is_masked_in_that_reply_and_on_later_screens` (card number in groups, then `4000-1234-…` on a later screen, then `+1 (KQX) WZ7-PL4`), `secrets::tests::a_field_that_shows_a_secret_changed_is_remembered_and_one_that_shows_other_text_is_not`.
- g453 3 (MCP save path): `mcp::savable`: a `.steps` file inside the server's folder (working directory; `Mcp::in_folder` sets it for tests), no `..`, no link, no parent a link takes outside, and only over a file that is already steps. A relative `file` is read from the same folder. Test `mcp.rs::replay_saves_only_a_steps_file_inside_its_folder_and_over_nothing_but_steps`; `a_save_that_cannot_write_leaves_the_steps_for_the_next_save` now blocks the write with a missing folder (the fixed `.tmp` it used is gone).
- g453 4c (sensitive flag on set): new `TextInput::SetSecret`; Android maps it to `set` with `sensitive`, and `TextInput.set()` passes the flag to the paste it falls back to. Jar rebuilt with `android-agent/build.sh`. Tests `hidden_display.rs::a_secret_set_on_the_hidden_display_keeps_its_sensitive_mark`, `map::tests::a_secret_set_as_the_value_is_marked_sensitive_for_the_paste_it_may_fall_back_to`. The Java path was not run on a device.
- g453 4d (escaped secret): the mask also matches the JSON-escaped and the quoted-string forms. Tests `daemon.rs::a_secret_with_quotes_and_backslashes_is_masked_where_a_reply_escapes_it` (snapshot), `secrets::tests::a_secret_is_masked_escaped_as_json_or_a_quoted_string_and_spaced_out` (a raw tree's JSON; the fake device has no tree capture).
- g453 5 (socket peer): `socket::connect` refuses a socket whose folder is not this user's or not 0700, and a peer whose euid is not ours (interprocess `peer_creds`, `libc::geteuid`; `libc` added as a Unix dependency, one line in `Cargo.lock`). Test `socket::tests::a_client_does_not_connect_through_a_folder_another_account_could_write_in`. The other-uid case is not testable without a second account.
- g453 6 (file modes): `private.rs`: screenshots in `tmp`, the `tmp/<device>` folders, a recording's steps file, the trace writer, `daemon.log` and a missing home are owner-only; the video crate's `Recording::create` makes 0600. Tests `daemon.rs::a_screenshot_the_daemon_keeps_can_be_read_only_by_its_owner`, `video/tests/record.rs::a_recording_can_be_read_only_by_its_owner`.
- #32 1: `{{name}}` skips `secret_env` (`summary` and `manual` sit outside `params` and were never filled). Test `variables::tests::the_variable_a_secret_is_read_from_is_not_filled`.
- #32 2: `replay-in-ci.md` and the reference say a value in a `shell` step is shell text.
- #32 3: a replayed non-`act` step drops the file's `device` and `share`. `save` needed no change: the journal builds each step's params from named fields and never holds either. Test `cli.rs::a_replayed_step_runs_as_the_replay_and_not_with_the_share_or_device_in_the_file`.

First runs, before the fix (`cargo test --locked --no-fail-fast -p phonedriver --test cli --test netview --test trace --test daemon --test hidden_display -- <names>`):
- cli: 5 failed. `replayed 1 step in 0.0 s / 1. shell echo shared` (share kept); `replayed 1 step … 1. typed into @e1` (secret into a plain field); `run.steps is not a plain file` (the symlinked `.tmp` was followed and renamed); `+ "hunter2-test-secret" at 1,1` in the replay error; `the daemon holds PHONEDRIVER_SECRET=s-plain`.
- daemon: 6 failed: `unknown method mask`; `expected an error` (secret typed into a plain field); the cut long secret, the escaped secret and the card number in the reply text; screenshot mode `left: 420` (0644).
- netview: 2 failed: `left: "log line: I/App( 1): token s3cr3t-c8-value"`; the cut line ending in the secret's first 10 characters.
- trace: `left: "echo otp-C10-778899"`. hidden_display: `left: [SetValue(9 chars)]`.
- The unit tests (`secrets`, `client`, `socket`, `variables`, `map`) and the MCP save test were written before the code they test but could not run red: the crate does not compile without the functions they call. The video mode test first failed through a stale build (below), not as a red run of the old code.

Runs after the fix, all on the WSL:
- `cargo fmt --all --check`: clean. `cargo clippy --locked --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`: clean (`Finished`).
- `cargo test --locked --workspace --no-fail-fast`: every binary `ok`, 0 failed (lib 150, cli 35, daemon 206, mcp 54, netview 13, trace 9, hidden_display 8, secret_property 3 + 1 ignored, guard 38, guard_property 7, core 238, android 84, ios 99, video lib 11, video record 6).
- A trap on the way: `wsl.sh` keeps the Mac's mtimes, and `record.rs` changed while the first build ran, so cargo kept an rlib built from the old file and the mode test failed in the workspace run. Touching the changed files on the WSL and running again fixed it.

Open for the lead: a Windows build of `detach.rs` (the new environment block) and a run that starts the daemon there; a device run of the Android `set` fallback with the sensitive flag; a person's look at whether `PHONEDRIVER_REPLAY_SECRET_ANY_FIELD` should also be readable from the daemon's environment.

## 2026-11-21: 0.1.7 security, the iPhone runner's channel and the Wi-Fi pairing (D106)

Branch `sec/ios-link`. Reports: GHSA-jg7g-xh43-x972 Part A, GHSA-mfp4-j74c-44xh items 2, 3 and 4, issue #32 item 8. Each was checked against `main` first and holds as written.

- **GHSA-jg7g-xh43-x972 Part A (runner with no authentication).** `runner::Token`: 32 bytes from `getrandom` per start, in the environment (`TEST_RUNNER_PHONEDRIVER_TOKEN`; `PHONEDRIVER_TOKEN` in the installed runner's XCTest configuration). The first frame is `hello` with a nonce and HMAC-SHA256 of `host:<nonce>`; the runner (`ios-runner/Runner/Auth.m`, `Server.m`) checks it with `timingsafe_bcmp`, refuses a nonce it has accepted, answers with its own proof of `runner:<nonce>`, and closes a connection that fails with one `unauthorized` reply, without touching the current one. First frame at most 4 KB before the check. Runner wire version 0.2.0. Reuse needs the token kept in `<home>/runner/<udid>.token` (0600, written through a rename); udid and build hash alone no longer count. Simulator build included.
- **GHSA-mfp4-j74c-44xh item 3 (Wi-Fi runner channel in clear).** Off a Mac (D103) the runner's port is opened inside the tunnel (`runner::DevicePort::Tunnel`, `adapter.connect(27753)`). On a Mac over Wi-Fi (D47) it stays plain TCP: the Mac's CoreDevice tunnel exists only while `xcodebuild` or `devicectl` holds the device, and whether the runner answers through it cannot be found out without a device. Documented in D106, `docs/reference.md`, `SECURITY.md` and the iPhone page, with "use the cable for secrets".
- **GHSA-mfp4-j74c-44xh item 2 (pair-verify does not authenticate the device).** idevice copy, branch `phonedriver-verify-device` in `/Users/hridyaagrawal/Honey/idevice-sec` (not pushed): `d97e9b6` keeps the device's Ed25519 key from pair-setup M6 (`RpPairingFile::device_public_key`; older records read with none) and verifies M2's encrypted signature before M3, failing closed (`NoDeviceKey`, `DeviceNotVerified`); a bad M6 is an error instead of a panic; reading a record no longer logs its private key at debug level. phonedriver: a record without the key gets `NeedsUser(PairForWifi)` before connecting (re-pair, not trust-on-first-use: the cable is the trusted channel); `ios wifi on` re-pairs through idevice's `connect` fallback. A peer that does not prove it is the device: `ios:<udid>: what answers at <addr> did not prove it is the device this computer paired with, so nothing was sent to it; ...`.
- **GHSA-mfp4-j74c-44xh item 4 (TSS over HTTP, redirects followed).** idevice `f580a6f`: `https://gs.apple.com/TSS/controller?action=2`, `HttpRequest::without_redirects`, a 3xx is an error. `curl -sI https://gs.apple.com/TSS/controller?action=2` from the MacBook (one request): `HTTP/1.1 401 Unauthorized`, `Server: Apple`, `Strict-Transport-Security: max-age=31536000; includeSubdomains`, certificate verified by curl. The privacy page's Apple row names the request.
- **Issue #32 item 8 (pairing record file).** `transport::file_udid` (letters, digits, `-`) and `transport::write_private` (new file + rename, skipped when unchanged, 0600 set on an existing file); used by `remote` and the token file.
- `crates/ios/Cargo.toml` pins idevice `f580a6f69e45e10e98fe6315e477697b75312332` and adds `hmac 0.13` and `getrandom 0.4`; `Cargo.lock` was resolved on the WSL against the fork by path and then given the git source line for that commit, so `--locked` works once the commit is on `hridaya423/idevice`.

- idevice, on the WSL (`cargo test -p idevice --no-default-features --features ring,remote_pairing,tss,usbmuxd --lib -- remote_pairing:: tss:: http::`): first run `error[E0609]: no field device_public_key`, `no variant ... DeviceNotVerified`, `NoDeviceKey`, `cannot find function device_key_from_m6`, `no method named without_redirects`, `no method named send_to` (15 errors). After: `test result: ok. 29 passed`. New: `a_device_that_signs_with_the_key_it_paired_with_is_verified`, `a_peer_that_signs_with_another_key_is_refused_before_this_host_signs_m3`, `a_signature_over_other_ephemeral_keys_is_refused`, `an_m2_without_encrypted_data_is_refused`, `a_record_without_the_device_key_is_refused_before_anything_is_sent`, `the_device_key_is_read_from_pair_setup_m6`, the record round trip and `recreate_signing_keys` tests, `a_request_that_does_not_follow_redirects_returns_the_redirect_and_never_reaches_its_target`, `the_tss_request_goes_to_apple_over_https`, `a_redirect_from_the_tss_server_is_an_error_and_its_target_is_never_contacted`. `cargo fmt -p idevice -- --check` clean; clippy with phonedriver's features, all targets: no warnings.

After: `ok. 26 passed`. New: `the_proofs_are_hmac_sha256_of_the_side_and_the_nonce_under_the_token` (vectors computed with Python's `hmac`, the same ones the runner's harness checks), `each_token_is_32_fresh_bytes_and_its_debug_form_hides_it`, `a_runner_that_proves_the_token_is_ours_and_the_token_never_crosses_the_wire`, `a_runner_started_with_another_token_refuses_the_connection_and_is_not_ours`, `a_listener_that_answers_with_the_right_udid_and_build_but_no_proof_is_not_ours`, `a_runner_is_reused_only_with_the_token_this_home_kept_for_it`, `a_kept_token_is_read_back_and_written_for_its_owner_only`, `through_the_remote_pairing_tunnel_the_runner_port_is_opened_inside_the_tunnel` (the adapter's first packet is an IPv6 TCP SYN to the device's tunnel address, port 27753), `a_record_made_before_the_device_key_was_kept_asks_for_the_cable_before_connecting`, `a_peer_that_does_not_prove_it_is_the_paired_device_is_named_as_such`, `a_udid_with_other_characters_never_becomes_a_path`, `a_record_is_replaced_whole_only_when_it_changed_and_is_left_for_its_owner_only`.

Now: `Ran 9 tests in 0.883s OK`. The old `0.0.0.0` listener also accepted `::1` on macOS, so the bind was left as it is (`test_the_listener_takes_ipv4_and_ipv6`).
- `xcodebuild -project PhonedriverRunner.xcodeproj -scheme PhonedriverRunner -destination 'generic/platform=iOS Simulator'` and `'generic/platform=iOS'` with `CODE_SIGNING_ALLOWED=NO -quiet build-for-testing` (Xcode 27.0, mini): no output, both `.xctestrun` files and `Auth.o` for arm64 and x86_64 (Simulator) and arm64 (device); the device binary holds the `unauthorized` message. Build folder deleted after; mini at 20 GB free before and after.

Commands on the WSL (`pd-sec-ios`, idevice switched to the local fork by path for the build): `cargo fmt --all --check` clean; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean (one `manual_is_multiple_of` fixed on the way); `cargo test -p phonedriver-ios -p phonedriver-core`: 238 and 111 passed (21, 4 and 5 ignored live tests); `cargo test -p phonedriver --lib --test daemon --test device_choice --test mcp --test cli`: 143, 30, 200, 7 and 53 passed.

Needs a device before release (fakes cannot show it):
- A runner on a phone answering inside the remote pairing's tunnel, off a Mac on Wi-Fi.

If the device's M2 differs from HAP's, every Wi-Fi tunnel off a Mac fails with "did not prove it is the device".

## 2026-11-21: a still screen records, and the Simulator's settings and log need no switch (D107, D108)

Two changes before 0.1.7 ships, both asked for by the user so that ordinary use needs no setting.

**Recordings of a still screen.** Since D107 a recording opened at the first keyframe after its start, and an idle
screen gives the encoder no input to make one from, so a recording of a screen that never changed had no picture.
Now the agent asks its encoder for a keyframe at the first repeat after a change (`Video.wrote`), and
`stream::still` starts the file from a kept group that is one keyframe and only its repeats (stamped exactly 100 ms
apart), which is the screen at the start and nothing older. Any other group waits for the keyframe as before.

- Test first: `a_recording_of_a_still_screen_opens_with_that_screen` failed with `the keyframe and its repeats,
  left: 0, right: 5`; after the change `cargo test -p phonedriver-video` (WSL): 11, 9, 6 and 15 passed, 0 failed.
  `a_recording_started_after_the_screen_changed_holds_no_picture_from_before_it` (renamed) still gives 0 frames for
  a kept group whose pictures changed.
- Emulator (win-pc, Pixel_API_37, software GPU, Windows debug build with the new agent jar): Settings opened, home
  pressed, 4 s idle, then `record start`/`stop` 3 s: `3.1 s, 570x1280, 7 frames, 10 kB`. `ffprobe`: the first packet
  is the keyframe; all 7 frames show the home screen (PSNR 47.3 to 48.0 dB against the first; looked at), no frame of
  Settings. Started right after `launch com.android.settings`: `3.3 s, 21 frames`, first frame the Settings list.

**The Simulator's `defaults` and `log`.** D108 refused every program in a Simulator without
`PHONEDRIVER_HOST_SHELL=1`. Now `defaults` (`read`, `read-type`, `write`, `delete`, `domains`, `find`, `rename`) and
`log show|stream` run as one program by their path in the Simulator, never through its shell; an argument starting
with `/` or `~` or holding `..`, a `defaults` domain with a `/`, `defaults import|export|-host`, other `log`
subcommands and log archives or files still need the switch, as does any other program or a pipe.

- Test first: `the_simulators_own_settings_and_log_need_no_switch` failed at the `unwrap` of a refusal, and the
  refusal test failed for not naming `defaults`; after: `cargo test -p phonedriver-ios --lib` 117 passed, 0 failed.
- `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean; `cargo fmt --all --check` clean after `cargo fmt`.
- Not run on a Simulator: the mini was not used for this change.
